We thank the Simons Center for Geometry and Physics (SCGP) at Stony Brook University for its hospitality and collaborative environment. This collaboration began at SCGP during the 3rd Simons Math Summer Workshop on “Partial Differential Equations of Classical Physics”, 7-25th July 2025.

2.
Equations for macroscopic quantities

In this section, equations are derived for macroscopic quantities of solutions of the Boltzmann equation. In Section
2.1
, we decompose the distribution
f
into its macroscopic and microscopic parts. In Section
2.2
, we introduce a set of suitable time-normalised orthonormal basis functions, and we show its orthonormality in Section
2.3
. Finally in Section
2.4
, we derive the macroscopic equations (
2.11
)–(
2.22
). Later in Section
4
, we will use (
2.11
)–(
2.22
) to estimate the part of the distribution
f
that is contained in the null space of the linearised collision operator. The calculations in this section hold for any
\mathfrak{q}\geq 0
.

2.1.
Macro-micro decomposition of the distribution

In this section, we decompose
f(t,x,p)
into its macroscopic part
{\bf P}f
and microscopic part
{\bf\{I-P\}}f
as

(2.1)

f={\bf P}f+{\bf\{I-P\}}f,

where
{\bf P}
is the orthogonal projection from
L^{2}_{p}({\mathbb{R}^{3}})
onto the null space of the linearised operator
\mathcal{L}
defined in (
1.21
). The operator
\mathcal{L}
has a 5-dimensional null space given by

\mathrm{nul}(\mathcal{L})=\mathrm{span}\Big\{t^{3\mathfrak{q}}J^{\frac{1}{2}}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p),\penalty\ \penalty\ t^{3\mathfrak{q}}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p^{i})J^{\frac{1}{2}}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p),\penalty\ \penalty\ t^{3\mathfrak{q}}|t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p|J^{\frac{1}{2}}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)\Big\},

where the linear span is taken over smooth functions of
t
and
x
. For convenience we work with an orthonormal basis of this space. Define the following functions

\displaystyle t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{1}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}

\displaystyle\penalty\ t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)},

(2.2)

\displaystyle t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{j+1}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}

\displaystyle\penalty\ \frac{1}{2}t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p^{j}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)},\qquad\text{for }\quad 1\leq j\leq 3,

\displaystyle t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{5}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}

\displaystyle\penalty\ \frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}|p|-3)t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)}.

We denote this set of functions as
\{t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)\}_{\ell=1}^{5}
.

Proposition 2.1
.

The set
\{t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)\}_{\ell=1}^{5}
forms an orthonormal basis with respect to
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{p}^{3})
of the space
\mathrm{nul}(\mathcal{L})
for all
t\geq 1
.

The orthonormal property of
\{t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)\}_{\ell=1}^{5}
is shown next in Section
2.3
. Then, we write
{\bf P}f
as a linear combination of the basis vectors above as

(2.3)

{\bf P}f=\left(\mathcal{A}^{f}+\sum_{j=1}^{3}\mathcal{B}^{f}_{j}t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p^{j}/2+\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}\mathcal{C}^{f}(\left|t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p\right|-3)\right)t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)},

where the coefficients are given by

(2.4)

\begin{split}\mathcal{A}^{f}(t,x)&=t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}f(t,x,p)\sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)}dp,\\
\mathcal{B}_{i}^{f}(t,x)&=\frac{t^{3\mathfrak{q}}}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}f(t,x,p)t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p^{i}\sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)}dp,\\
\mathcal{C}^{f}(t,x)&=\frac{t^{3\mathfrak{q}}}{\sqrt{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}^{3}}}f(t,x,p)(|t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p|-3)\sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)}dp.\end{split}

2.2.
Orthonormal time-normalised basis

As will be seen in Section
2.4
, when the transport operator
\partial_{t}+\frac{p^{i}}{|t^{\mathfrak{q}}p|}\partial_{x^{i}}-\frac{2\mathfrak{q}}{t}p^{i}\partial_{p^{i}}
acts on the span of the null space elements

(2.5)

t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)},\qquad t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\frac{t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p^{i}}{2}\sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)},\qquad\frac{t^{3\mathfrak{q}}}{\sqrt{3}}(|t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p|-3)\sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)}

over smooth functions of
t
and
x
, the result lives in a 13-dimensional space. We now extend the orthonormal velocity functions
\{t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)\}_{\ell=1}^{5}
in (
2.2
). To this end, for
1\leq l,j\leq 3
we define the additional orthonormal velocity functions

(2.6)

\begin{split}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{j+5}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}&\penalty\ \sqrt{\frac{3}{7}}\left(\frac{p^{j}}{{|p|}}-t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p^{j}\right)t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)},\qquad\text{for }\quad 1\leq j\leq 3,\\
t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{9}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}&\penalty\ {t^{2\mathfrak{q}}}\frac{\sqrt{5}}{2}\left(\frac{(p^{1})^{2}-(p^{3})^{2}}{{|p|}}\right)t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)},\\
t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{10}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}&\penalty\ {t^{2\mathfrak{q}}}\frac{\sqrt{5}}{2}\left(\frac{(p^{1})^{2}+(p^{3})^{2}-2(p^{2})^{2}}{{|p|}}\right)t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)},\\
t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{i+j+8}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}&\penalty\ \frac{\sqrt{5}}{2}{t^{2\mathfrak{q}}}\frac{p^{l}p^{j}}{|p|}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)},\qquad\text{for }\quad 1\leq l<j\leq 3.\end{split}

We denote this set of
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{p})
orthonormal functions with (
2.2
) as
\{t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)\}_{\ell=1}^{13}
.

We remark that the diagonal of the symmetric tensor
\frac{p^{l}p^{j}-\frac{1}{3}|p|^{2}}{|p|}
vanishes

\displaystyle\frac{(p^{1})^{2}-\frac{1}{3}|p|^{2}}{{|p|}}+\frac{(p^{2})^{2}-\frac{1}{3}|p|^{2}}{{|p|}}+\frac{(p^{3})^{2}-\frac{1}{3}|p|^{2}}{{|p|}}=0.

Thus the diagonal only contains two independent components that we represent in the components of
t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{9}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)
and
t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{10}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)
. In this system of orthonormal functions, (
2.2
) with (
2.6
),
\mathfrak{e}_{1}
is the density mode,
\mathfrak{e}_{j+1}
are the three momentum modes,
\mathfrak{e}_{5}
is the energy mode,
\mathfrak{e}_{j+5}
are the three heat-flux modes,
\mathfrak{e}_{9}
and
\mathfrak{e}_{10}
are the two independent diagonal components of the stress tensor modes,
and
\mathfrak{e}_{i+j+8}
are the three off-diagonal stress tensor modes. These orthonormal functions in (
2.2
) and (
2.6
) are motivated by the Grad 13-moment system introduced in
[
35
]
in 1949, and also by the polynomial momentum basis used in
[
80
,
45
]
for the relativistic Boltzmann equation in the case of massive particles.

Proposition 2.2
.

The set
\{t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)\}_{\ell=1}^{13}
forms an orthonormal family with respect to
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{p}^{3})
for all
t\geq 1
.

The orthonormal property of
\{t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)\}_{\ell=1}^{13}
is shown later in Section
2.3
.

2.3.
Orthonormal property of the bases

In this section, we prove Proposition
2.2
, in other words, we show that
\{t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)\}_{\ell=1}^{13}
is an orthonormal basis with respect to
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{p}^{3})
. This follows from some elementary integral computations that we write below.

It is well-known for the Gamma function
\Gamma
that
\int_{0}^{\infty}\rho^{n}e^{-\rho}d\rho=n!
for all
n\in\{0,1,2,\ldots\}
. Hence

(2.7)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|p|^{n}\frac{e^{-|p|}}{8\pi}dp=\frac{(n+2)!}{2},\qquad\forall n\in\{0,1,2,\ldots\}.

Recall the definition of
J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)
in (
1.16
). We use the integral (
2.7
) to calculate the values of various integrals related to
J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)
as in the following for
1\leq a\leq 3
:

\displaystyle t^{6\mathfrak{q}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}^{3}}}J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)dp

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{e^{-|p|}}{8\pi}dp=1,

\displaystyle t^{6\mathfrak{q}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p|J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)dp

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|p|\frac{e^{-|p|}}{8\pi}dp=3,

(2.8)

\displaystyle t^{6\mathfrak{q}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p|^{2}J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)dp

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|p|^{2}\frac{e^{-|p|}}{8\pi}dp=12,

\displaystyle t^{6\mathfrak{q}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p^{a})^{2}J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)dp

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(p^{1})^{2}\frac{e^{-|p|}}{8\pi}dp=\frac{1}{3}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|p|^{2}\frac{e^{-|p|}}{8\pi}dp=4,

\displaystyle t^{6\mathfrak{q}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p^{a})^{2}}{{|t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p|}}J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)dp

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{(p^{1})^{2}}{{|p|}}\frac{e^{-|p|}}{8\pi}dp=\frac{1}{3}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|p|\frac{e^{-|p|}}{8\pi}dp=1,

\displaystyle t^{6\mathfrak{q}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p|-3)^{2}J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)dp

\displaystyle=t^{-6\mathfrak{q}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|p|-3)^{2}\frac{e^{-|p|}}{8\pi}dp=3.

From the integrals above, the functions
\{t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)\}_{\ell=1}^{5}
have unit
L^{2}_{p}
norm. Moreover, one can easily show that the functions in
\{t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)\}_{\ell=1}^{5}
are orthogonal. This shows Proposition
2.1
.

Together with (
2.8
) we also use the values of the following integrals for
1\leq a\leq 3
:

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|p|^{3}\frac{e^{-|p|}}{8\pi}dp

\displaystyle=60,

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}^{3}}}\frac{(p^{a})^{2}}{{|p|}}({|p|}-3)\frac{e^{-|p|}}{8\pi}dp

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{3}\int_{{\mathbb{R}^{3}}}{|p|}({|p|}-3)\frac{e^{-|p|}}{8\pi}dp=1,

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}^{3}}}\frac{(p^{a})^{2}}{{|p|}}({|p|}-4)\frac{e^{-|p|}}{8\pi}dp

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{3}\int_{{\mathbb{R}^{3}}}{|p|}({|p|}-4)\frac{e^{-|p|}}{8\pi}dp=0,

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}^{3}}}({|p|}-3)({|p|}-4)\frac{e^{-|p|}}{8\pi}dp

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbb{R}^{3}}}({|p|}^{2}-7{|p|}+12)\frac{e^{-|p|}}{8\pi}dp=3.

We also compute for
1\leq a\leq 3
that

\displaystyle t^{6\mathfrak{q}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{(p^{a})^{2}}{{|p|^{2}}}J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)dp

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{(p^{1})^{2}}{{|p|^{2}}}\frac{e^{-|p|}}{8\pi}dp=\frac{1}{3}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{e^{-|p|}}{8\pi}dp=\frac{1}{3},

and also the values of the integrals
\int_{{\mathbb{R}^{3}}}\frac{(p^{a})^{2}(p^{b})^{2}}{{|p|^{2}}}\frac{e^{-|p|}}{8\pi}dp
for
1\leq a
,
b\leq 3
, given by

\begin{split}\int_{{\mathbb{R}^{3}}}\frac{(p^{a})^{2}(p^{b})^{2}}{{|p|^{2}}}\frac{e^{-|p|}}{8\pi}dp&=\frac{4}{5},\qquad\text{for}\quad a\neq b,\\
\int_{{\mathbb{R}^{3}}}\frac{(p^{a})^{2}(p^{a})^{2}}{{|p|^{2}}}\frac{e^{-|p|}}{8\pi}dp&=\frac{12}{5}.\end{split}

For the basis elements
\left(\sqrt{\frac{3}{7}}\left(\frac{p^{a}}{{|p|}}-t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p^{a}\right)t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\sqrt{J}\right)_{1\leq a\leq 3}
considered in (
2.6
), we have

t^{6\mathfrak{q}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(\frac{p^{a}}{{|p|}}-t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p^{a}\right)^{2}J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)dp=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(\frac{p^{a}}{{|p|}}-p^{a}\right)^{2}\frac{e^{-|p|}}{8\pi}dp\\
=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(\frac{\left(p^{a}\right)^{2}}{{|p|^{2}}}+\left(p^{a}\right)^{2}-2\frac{\left(p^{a}\right)^{2}}{{|p|}}\right)\frac{e^{-|p|}}{8\pi}dp=\frac{7}{3}.

The computations above, and similar ones, show the functions
\{t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)\}_{\ell=1}^{13}
have unit
L^{2}_{p}
norm. One can easily show that the functions in
\{t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)\}_{\ell=1}^{13}
are also
L^{2}_{p}
orthogonal. This proves Proposition
2.2
.

2.4.
Macroscopic Equations

In this section we derive the macroscopic equations for the coefficients
\mathcal{A}^{f}
,
\mathcal{B}^{f}
, and
\mathcal{C}^{f}
in (
2.4
), when
f
is a solution of the Boltzmann equation. For given function
f
, define for
\ell\in\{1,2,\ldots,13\}
the quantities

(2.9)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathfrak{m}_{\ell}\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}&\penalty\ \left\langle{{\bf\{I-P\}}f},{t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)}\right\rangle,\\
\Theta_{\ell}\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}&\penalty\ \left\langle{{\bf\{I-P\}}f},{t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\frac{p}{|p|}\mathfrak{e}_{\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)}\right\rangle,\\
\mathfrak{l}_{\ell}\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}&\penalty\ -\left\langle{\mathcal{L}({\bf\{I-P\}}f)},{t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)}\right\rangle,\\
\mathfrak{h}_{\ell}\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}&\penalty\ \left\langle{\Gamma(f,f)},{t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)}\right\rangle,\\
\mathfrak{u}_{\ell}\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}&\penalty\ -t^{-\mathfrak{q}}\nabla_{x}\cdot\Theta_{\ell}+\mathfrak{l}_{\ell}+\mathfrak{h}_{\ell},\end{aligned}

where
\nabla_{x}\penalty\ \cdot
is the standard
\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}
divergence of a vector field.

Proposition 2.3

(Macroscopic equations)
.

If
f
solves the Boltzmann equation (
1.19
), and
\mathcal{A}
,
\mathcal{B}
, and
\mathcal{C}
are defined by (
2.4
), then the following local conservation laws are satisfied

(2.10)

\displaystyle t^{-3\mathfrak{q}}\partial_{t}\left(t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathcal{A}\right)+\frac{1}{2}t^{-\mathfrak{q}}\nabla_{x}\cdot\mathcal{B}

\displaystyle=-t^{-\mathfrak{q}}\nabla_{x}\cdot\Theta_{1},

\displaystyle t^{-3\mathfrak{q}}\partial_{t}\left(t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathcal{B}_{j}\right)+2t^{-\mathfrak{q}}\partial_{j}\left(\mathcal{A}-\frac{2}{\sqrt{3}}\mathcal{C}\right)

\displaystyle=-t^{-\mathfrak{q}}\nabla_{x}\cdot\Theta_{j+1},\quad(1\leq j\leq 3),

\displaystyle t^{-3\mathfrak{q}}\partial_{t}\left(t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathcal{C}\right)+\frac{1}{2\sqrt{3}}t^{-\mathfrak{q}}\nabla_{x}\cdot\mathcal{B}

\displaystyle=-t^{-\mathfrak{q}}\nabla_{x}\cdot\Theta_{5},

and also the macroscopic equations (for
1\leq l,j\leq 3
),

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{7}{3}}t^{-\mathfrak{q}}\left(\partial_{j}\mathcal{A}-\sqrt{3}\partial_{j}\mathcal{C}\right)

\displaystyle=-t^{-3\mathfrak{q}}\partial_{t}\left(t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{m}_{j+5}\right)+\mathfrak{u}_{j+5},

(2.11)

\displaystyle t^{-\mathfrak{q}}\frac{1}{2\sqrt{5}}\left(\partial_{1}\mathcal{B}_{1}-\partial_{3}\mathcal{B}_{3}\right)

\displaystyle=-t^{-3\mathfrak{q}}\partial_{t}\left(t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{m}_{9}\right)+\mathfrak{u}_{9},

\displaystyle t^{-\mathfrak{q}}\frac{1}{6\sqrt{5}}\left(\nabla_{x}\cdot\mathcal{B}-3\partial_{2}\mathcal{B}_{2}\right)

\displaystyle=-t^{-3\mathfrak{q}}\partial_{t}\left(t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{m}_{10}\right)+\mathfrak{u}_{10},

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{5}}t^{-\mathfrak{q}}\left(\partial_{j}\mathcal{B}_{l}+\partial_{l}\mathcal{B}_{j}\right)

\displaystyle=-t^{-3\mathfrak{q}}\partial_{t}\left(t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{m}_{l+j+9}\right)+\mathfrak{u}_{l+j+9},\quad\text{for}\quad(l\neq j),

where the fixed indices above are
l,j\in\{1,2,3\}
.

Proof of Proposition
2.3
.

Let us plug the decomposition (
2.1
) into (
1.19
), then we obtain

(2.12)

\left\{\partial_{t}+\frac{p^{i}}{|t^{\mathfrak{q}}p|}\partial_{x^{i}}-\frac{2\mathfrak{q}}{t}p^{i}\partial_{p^{i}}\right\}{\bf P}f=-\left\{\partial_{t}-\frac{2\mathfrak{q}}{t}p^{i}\partial_{p^{i}}\right\}{\bf\{I-P\}}f\\
-\frac{p^{i}}{|t^{\mathfrak{q}}p|}\partial_{x^{i}}({\bf\{I-P\}}f)-\mathcal{L}({\bf\{I-P\}}f)+\Gamma(f,f).

We also recall
\mathcal{L}(f)=\mathcal{L}({\bf\{I-P\}}f)
by the definition of
{\bf P}
in (
2.3
). We have expressed here the macroscopic part
{\bf P}f
in terms of the microscopic part
{\bf\{I-P\}}f
and the non-linear term
\Gamma(f,f)
.

We consider the expression (
2.3
) of
{\bf P}f
with respect to the basis functions (
2.2
). Also using (
1.9
), we obtain that the left-hand side of (
2.12
) can be written as

(2.13)

\partial_{t}\left(t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathcal{A}\right)\sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)}+\partial_{t}\left(t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathcal{C}\right)({|t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p|}-3)\sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)}/\sqrt{3}\\
+t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\sum_{j=1}^{3}\partial_{j}(\mathcal{A}+\mathcal{C}({|t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p|}-3)/\sqrt{3})\frac{p^{j}}{{|t^{\mathfrak{q}}p|}}\sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)}\\
+\sum_{j=1}^{3}\partial_{t}\left(t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathcal{B}_{j}\right)\frac{t^{2\mathfrak{q}}}{2}p^{j}\sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)}+\sum_{l,j=1}^{3}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\partial_{j}\mathcal{B}_{l}\frac{t^{2\mathfrak{q}}}{2}\frac{p^{l}p^{j}}{{|t^{\mathfrak{q}}p|}}\sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)},

where
\mathcal{A}=\mathcal{A}^{f}
,
\mathcal{B}=\mathcal{B}^{f}
,
\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{C}^{f}
and
\partial_{j}=\partial_{x_{j}}
. We rewrite (
2.13
) as

(2.14)

t^{-3\mathfrak{q}}\partial_{t}\left(t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathcal{A}\right)t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)}+t^{-3\mathfrak{q}}\partial_{t}\left(t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathcal{C}\right)({|t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p|}-3)t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)}/\sqrt{3}\\
+\sum_{i=1}^{3}t^{-\mathfrak{q}}\partial_{i}\mathcal{A}\frac{p^{i}}{{|p|}}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)}+\sum_{i=1}^{3}t^{-\mathfrak{q}}\partial_{i}\mathcal{C}\frac{p^{i}}{{|p|}}({|t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p|}-3)t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)}/\sqrt{3}\\
+\sum_{i=1}^{3}t^{-3\mathfrak{q}}\partial_{t}\left(t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathcal{B}_{i}\right)\frac{t^{2\mathfrak{q}}}{2}p^{i}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)}+\sum_{i,j=1}^{3}t^{-\mathfrak{q}}\partial_{j}\mathcal{B}_{i}\frac{t^{2\mathfrak{q}}}{2}\frac{p^{i}p^{j}}{{|p|}}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)}\\
\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}\mathcal{Z}_{1}+\mathcal{Z}_{2}+\mathcal{Z}_{3}.

Here

(2.15)

\mathcal{Z}_{1}\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}t^{-3\mathfrak{q}}\partial_{t}\left(t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathcal{A}\right)t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)}+t^{-3\mathfrak{q}}\partial_{t}\left(t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathcal{C}\right)({|t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p|}-3)t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)}/\sqrt{3}\\
+t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{1}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)\frac{t^{-\mathfrak{q}}}{2}\nabla_{x}\cdot\mathcal{B}+t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{5}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)\frac{t^{-\mathfrak{q}}}{2\sqrt{3}}\nabla_{x}\cdot\mathcal{B}.

Thus

(2.16)

\mathcal{Z}_{1}=t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{1}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)\left(t^{-3\mathfrak{q}}\partial_{t}\left(t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathcal{A}\right)+\frac{1}{2}t^{-\mathfrak{q}}\nabla_{x}\cdot\mathcal{B}\right)\\
+t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{5}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)\left(t^{-3\mathfrak{q}}\partial_{t}\left(t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathcal{C}\right)+\frac{1}{2\sqrt{3}}t^{-\mathfrak{q}}\nabla_{x}\cdot\mathcal{B}\right).

The above is our main expression for (
2.16
) and it leads directly to (
2.10
)
1
and (
2.10
)
3
.

Next we consider
\mathcal{Z}_{3}
, but first we notice that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{1}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)+\frac{1}{2\sqrt{3}}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{5}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)=\frac{t^{2\mathfrak{q}}}{6}|p|t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)}=\frac{t^{2\mathfrak{q}}}{2}\frac{\frac{1}{3}|p|^{2}}{|p|}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)}.

Thus we expand

\mathcal{Z}_{3}\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}\sum_{i,j=1}^{3}t^{-\mathfrak{q}}\partial_{j}\mathcal{B}_{i}\frac{t^{2\mathfrak{q}}}{2}\frac{p^{i}p^{j}}{{|p|}}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)}-\frac{t^{2\mathfrak{q}}}{2}\frac{\frac{1}{3}|p|^{2}}{|p|}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)}t^{-\mathfrak{q}}\nabla_{x}\cdot\mathcal{B}\\
=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j=1\\
i<j\end{subarray}}^{3}t^{-\mathfrak{q}}\left(\partial_{j}\mathcal{B}^{i}+\partial_{i}\mathcal{B}_{j}\right)\frac{t^{2\mathfrak{q}}}{2}\frac{p^{i}p^{j}}{{|p|}}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)}\\
+\sum_{j=1}^{3}t^{-\mathfrak{q}}\partial_{j}\mathcal{B}_{j}\frac{t^{2\mathfrak{q}}}{2}\frac{(p^{j})^{2}-\frac{1}{3}|p|^{2}}{{|p|}}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)}.

For the ortho-normal functions in (
2.6
) we have

\displaystyle\frac{t^{2\mathfrak{q}}}{2}\frac{(p^{1})^{2}-\frac{1}{3}|p|^{2}}{{|p|}}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)}

\displaystyle=\frac{t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{9}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)}{2\sqrt{5}}+\frac{t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{10}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)}{6\sqrt{5}},

\displaystyle\frac{t^{2\mathfrak{q}}}{2}\frac{(p^{2})^{2}-\frac{1}{3}|p|^{2}}{{|p|}}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)}

\displaystyle=-\frac{t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{10}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)}{3\sqrt{5}},

\displaystyle\frac{t^{2\mathfrak{q}}}{2}\frac{(p^{3})^{2}-\frac{1}{3}|p|^{2}}{{|p|}}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)}

\displaystyle=-\frac{t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{9}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)}{2\sqrt{5}}+\frac{t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{10}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)}{6\sqrt{5}}.

Thus we express
\mathcal{Z}_{3}
in terms of the orthonormal functions as

(2.17)

\mathcal{Z}_{3}=t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{9}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)\left(t^{-\mathfrak{q}}\frac{\partial_{1}\mathcal{B}_{1}-\partial_{3}\mathcal{B}_{3}}{2\sqrt{5}}\right)+t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{10}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)\left(t^{-\mathfrak{q}}\frac{\nabla_{x}\cdot\mathcal{B}-3\partial_{2}\mathcal{B}_{2}}{6\sqrt{5}}\right)\\
+\frac{1}{\sqrt{5}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j=1\\
l<j\end{subarray}}^{3}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{i+j+9}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)t^{-\mathfrak{q}}\left(\partial_{j}\mathcal{B}^{i}+\partial_{i}\mathcal{B}_{j}\right).

This is our main expression for
\mathcal{Z}_{3}
and it leads directly to (
2.11
)
2
-(
2.11
)
4
.

Next we consider
\mathcal{Z}_{2}
in (
2.14
) given by

(2.18)

\mathcal{Z}_{2}\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}\sum_{i=1}^{3}t^{-\mathfrak{q}}\partial_{i}\mathcal{A}\frac{p^{i}}{{|p|}}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)}+\sum_{i=1}^{3}t^{-\mathfrak{q}}\partial_{i}\mathcal{C}\frac{p^{i}}{{|p|}}({|t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p|}-3)t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)}/\sqrt{3}\\
+\sum_{i=1}^{3}t^{-3\mathfrak{q}}\partial_{t}\left(t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathcal{B}_{i}\right)\frac{t^{2\mathfrak{q}}}{2}p^{i}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)}.

From (
2.6
) we have

\displaystyle\frac{p^{j}}{{|p|}}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)}=\sqrt{\frac{7}{3}}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{j+5}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)+2t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{j+1}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p),

and

\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}\frac{p^{j}}{{|p|}}({|t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p|}-3)t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)}=\frac{2}{\sqrt{3}}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{j+1}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)-\sqrt{3}\frac{p^{j}}{{|p|}}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)}\\
=\frac{2}{\sqrt{3}}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{j+1}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)-\sqrt{7}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{j+5}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)-2\sqrt{3}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{j+1}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)\\
=-\sqrt{7}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{j+5}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)-\frac{4}{\sqrt{3}}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{j+1}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p).

Thus

(2.19)

\mathcal{Z}_{2}=\sqrt{\frac{7}{3}}\sum_{j=1}^{3}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{j+5}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)t^{-\mathfrak{q}}\partial_{j}\left(\mathcal{A}-\sqrt{3}\mathcal{C}\right)\\
+\sum_{j=1}^{3}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{j+1}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)\left(t^{-3\mathfrak{q}}\partial_{t}\left(t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathcal{B}_{j}\right)+2t^{-\mathfrak{q}}\partial_{j}\left(\mathcal{A}-\frac{2}{\sqrt{3}}\mathcal{C}\right)\right).

This equation (
2.19
) is our main expression for
\mathcal{Z}_{2}
.

We now consider the first term on the right side of (
2.12
). Multiplying by the individual basis functions in
\{t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)\}_{\ell=1}^{13}
in (
2.6
), then integrating and using (
1.9
),
we get

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}dp\,t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)\penalty\

\displaystyle\left\{\partial_{t}-\frac{2\mathfrak{q}}{t}p^{i}\partial_{p^{i}}\right\}{\bf\{I-P\}}f

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}dp\penalty\ \left\{\partial_{t}-\frac{2\mathfrak{q}}{t}p^{i}\partial_{p^{i}}\right\}\left(t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p){\bf\{I-P\}}f\right)

\displaystyle\penalty\ -\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}dp\penalty\ 3\mathfrak{q}t^{3\mathfrak{q}-1}\mathfrak{e}_{\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p){\bf\{I-P\}}f

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \left(\partial_{t}+\frac{3\mathfrak{q}}{t}\right)\left\langle{{\bf\{I-P\}}f},{t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)}\right\rangle.

Thus from (
1.49
) we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}dp\penalty\ t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)\left\{\partial_{t}-\frac{2\mathfrak{q}}{t}p^{i}\partial_{p^{i}}\right\}{\bf\{I-P\}}f=

\displaystyle\penalty\ t^{-3\mathfrak{q}}\partial_{t}\left(t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\langle{\bf\{I-P\}}f,t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)\rangle\right).

And we also have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}dp\penalty\ t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)

\displaystyle\frac{p}{|t^{\mathfrak{q}}p|}\cdot\nabla_{x}{\bf\{I-P\}}f=t^{-\mathfrak{q}}\nabla_{x}\cdot\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}dp\penalty\ \left(t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\frac{p}{|p|}\mathfrak{e}_{\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p){\bf\{I-P\}}f\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ t^{-\mathfrak{q}}\nabla_{x}\cdot\left\langle{{\bf\{I-P\}}f},{t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\frac{p}{|p|}\mathfrak{e}_{\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)}\right\rangle.

We recall now the definitions (
2.9
) of
\mathfrak{m}_{\ell},

\Theta_{\ell}
,
\mathfrak{l}_{\ell}
,
\mathfrak{h}_{\ell}
, and
\mathfrak{u}_{\ell}
. We observe that
\mathfrak{m}_{j}=\mathfrak{l}_{j}=\mathfrak{h}_{j}=0
for
1\leq j\leq 5
. Indeed, the linearised term
\mathfrak{l}_{\ell}
vanishes because of the self-adjointness of
\mathcal{L}
, and the nonlinear term
\mathfrak{h}_{\ell}
can be shown to vanish using the definition of
\Gamma(f,f)
and
p^{0}+q^{0}=p^{\prime 0}+q^{\prime 0}
. This enables us to obtain the local conservation laws in (
2.10
) directly from the microscopic equations.

Multiplying (
2.12
) in the form (
2.14
)–(
2.16
)–(
2.19
) by each basis function in
\{t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)\}_{\ell=1}^{13}
defined in (
2.6
) and integrating over
{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{p}
, we obtain the stated local conservation laws and macroscopic equations for the coefficients
\mathcal{A}
,
\mathcal{B}^{i}
, and
\mathcal{C}
.
∎

The equations (
2.10
) directly imply the following conservation laws for the coefficients
\mathcal{A}
,
\mathcal{B}
,
\mathcal{C}
:

Corollary 2.4

(Conserved quantities)
.

From the system of conservation laws (
2.10
), we obtain for every
j\in\{1,2,3\}
and all
t\geq 1
that

\displaystyle t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\mathcal{A}(t,x)dx=t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\mathcal{B}_{j}(t,x)dx=t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\mathcal{C}(t,x)dx=0.

Equivalently, for all
t\geq 1
, we have

(2.20)

\widehat{[\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B},\mathcal{C}]}(t,0)=\widehat{[\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B},\mathcal{C}]}(1,0)=0,

where
[\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B},\mathcal{C}]
denotes the vector with coefficients
\mathcal{A}
,
\mathcal{B}^{i}
, and
\mathcal{C}
.

Remark 2.5
.

Note that we have assumed in our main results that condition (
2.20
) holds initially at
t=1
.

For later use, we rewrite the local conservation laws in (
2.10
) as

(2.21)

\begin{split}\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}+\frac{1}{2}t^{-\mathfrak{q}}\nabla_{x}\cdot\mathcal{B}+\frac{3\mathfrak{q}}{t}{\mathcal{A}}&=-t^{-\mathfrak{q}}\nabla_{x}\cdot\Theta_{1},\\
\partial_{t}\mathcal{B}_{j}+2t^{-\mathfrak{q}}\partial_{j}\left(\mathcal{A}-\frac{2}{\sqrt{3}}\mathcal{C}\right)+\frac{3\mathfrak{q}}{t}{\mathcal{B}_{j}}&=-t^{-\mathfrak{q}}\nabla_{x}\cdot\Theta_{j+1},\quad\text{for}\quad 1\leq j\leq 3,\\
\partial_{t}\mathcal{C}+\frac{1}{2\sqrt{3}}t^{-\mathfrak{q}}\nabla_{x}\cdot\mathcal{B}+\frac{3\mathfrak{q}}{t}{\mathcal{C}}&=-t^{-\mathfrak{q}}\nabla_{x}\cdot\Theta_{5}.\end{split}

We next subtract
2\sqrt{\frac{3}{7}}
times (
2.11
)
1
from (
2.10
)
2
to obtain instead

(2.22)

\displaystyle t^{-3\mathfrak{q}}\partial_{t}\left(t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathcal{B}_{j}\right)+t^{-\mathfrak{q}}\frac{2}{\sqrt{3}}\partial_{j}\mathcal{C}

\displaystyle=-t^{-3\mathfrak{q}}\partial_{t}\left(t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{m}_{j+13}\right)+\mathfrak{u}_{j+13}\quad(1\leq j\leq 3),

where

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}_{j+13}\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}-2\sqrt{\frac{3}{7}}\mathfrak{m}_{j+5},\qquad\mathfrak{u}_{j+13}\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}-t^{-\mathfrak{q}}\nabla_{x}\cdot\Theta_{j+1}-2\sqrt{\frac{3}{7}}\mathfrak{u}_{j+5}.

We will use this system (
2.11
), (
2.21
) and (
2.22
) in Section
4
to obtain the crucial time-normalised coercivity estimate.

3.
Estimates for the collision operator

In this section, estimates for the linear and nonlinear parts of the collision operator are obtained. In Section
3.1
, we will estimate the integral operator
\mathcal{K}
defined in (
1.39
). In Section
3.2
, we show the main coercivity estimate for the linearised operator
\mathcal{L}
. In Section
3.3
, we prove weighted trilinear estimates for the nonlinear term
\Gamma(f,f)
. Finally, we show in Section
3.4
estimates for averaged quantities in the momentum variables against the basis functions in (
2.6
). The estimates in this section hold for any
\mathfrak{q}\geq 0
.

3.1.
Estimates for the integral momentum operator
\mathcal{K}

In this section, we show that the operator
\mathcal{K}
can be decomposed into a compact and a small part. This is the main step to prove the coercivity for the linearised operator
\mathcal{L}
.

Proposition 3.1

(Compact-small decomposition of
\mathcal{K}
)
.

Let
m\geq 0
and
w_{m}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)
as in (
1.41
). For any small
\eta>0,
we can decompose
\mathcal{K}
from (
1.39
) as

\mathcal{K}=\mathcal{K}_{c}+\mathcal{K}_{s},

where we have that for all
t\geq 1
,

t^{3\mathfrak{q}}|\langle w_{m}^{2}\mathcal{K}_{s}h_{1},h_{2}\rangle|\leq\eta\|w_{m}h_{1}\|_{L^{2}_{p}}\|w_{m}h_{2}\|_{L^{2}_{p}}.

for any suitably regular
h_{1}
and
h_{2}
. Furthermore, for some
R=R(\eta)>0
chosen sufficiently large, and for any
0<\zeta<1
there is a large constant
C=C_{\eta,\zeta}>0
such that for all
t\geq 1
,

(3.1)

\displaystyle t^{3\mathfrak{q}}|\langle w_{m}^{2}\mathcal{K}_{c}h_{1},h_{2}\rangle|\leq Ct^{-3\zeta\mathfrak{q}}\|{\bf 1}_{\leq R}h_{1}\|_{L^{2}_{p}}\|{\bf 1}_{\leq R}h_{2}\|_{L^{2}_{p}},

where
{\bf 1}_{\leq R}
is the indicator function of the ball
B_{R}
.
In addition
\mathcal{K}_{c}
is a compact operator on
L^{2}_{p}
.

Corollary 3.2
.

Let
m\geq 0
and
w_{m}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)
as in (
1.41
). For any
R>0
large enough, there is some
C_{R}>0
such that for all suitably regular
f
, and for all
t\geq 1
,

(3.2)

t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\left\langle{w_{m}^{2}\mathcal{L}f},{f}\right\rangle\gtrsim\big\|w_{m}f\big\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})}-C_{R}\big\|{\bf 1}_{\leq R}f\big\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{p}}.

Corollary
3.2
follows directly from Proposition
3.1
. The estimate (
3.2
) is similar to
[
45
, Section 2.6, Lemma 2.9]
for the relativistic Boltzmann equation on
\mathbb{T}^{3}_{x}
in the massive case.

Proof of Proposition
3.1
.

We write the linear operators
\mathcal{K}_{1}
and
\mathcal{K}_{2}
from (
1.39
) in terms of a kernel as

(3.3)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{j}h=t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\penalty\ dq\penalty\ k_{j}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p,t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)\penalty\ h(q),\qquad\text{for}\penalty\ \penalty\ j\in\{1,2\}.

Here, from (
1.31
) and (
1.39
) for
k_{1}=k_{1}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p,t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)
we have

(3.4)

k_{1}\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}\frac{1}{2}\left(1-\frac{p\cdot q}{|p||q|}\right)e^{-t^{2\mathfrak{q}}(|p|+|q|)/2}=\frac{1}{4}\left|\frac{p}{|p|}-\frac{q}{|q|}\right|^{2}e^{-t^{2\mathfrak{q}}(|p|+|q|)/2},

and
k_{2}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p,t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)
is defined in (
3.10
) below. We first prove the estimates for
\mathcal{K}_{1}
.

Given
R\geq 1
, we set a positive and smooth cut-off function
\phi_{R}=\phi_{R}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p,t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)
such that

(3.5)

\begin{split}\phi_{R}=1,\quad\text{if}\quad t^{2\mathfrak{q}}|p|+t^{2\mathfrak{q}}|q|\leq R/2,\qquad\left|\phi_{R}\right|\leq 1,\\
\text{supp}(\phi_{R})\subset\left\{(p,q)\left|\penalty\ t^{2\mathfrak{q}}|p|+t^{2\mathfrak{q}}|q|\leq R\right.\right\}.\end{split}

We use this cut-off function with several different
R
values in the rest of the proof.

First we estimate
k_{1}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p,t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)
in (
1.39
)
and (
3.3
). For large
R_{1}>0
we split

\displaystyle k_{1}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p,t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)

\displaystyle=\phi_{R_{1}}k_{1}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p,t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)+(1-\phi_{R_{1}})k_{1}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p,t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)

\displaystyle\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}k_{1c}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p,t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)+k_{1s}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p,t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q),

and then we similarly decompose

(3.6)

\mathcal{K}_{1}=\mathcal{K}_{1c}+\mathcal{K}_{1s}.

Recall now, for
m\geq 0
, the weight
w_{m}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)
in (
1.41
). From (
3.4
) and (
3.5
) there is a uniform constant such that

\displaystyle w_{m}^{2}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)\left|k_{1s}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p,t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)\right|\lesssim e^{-R_{1}/4}e^{-t^{2\mathfrak{q}}(|p|+|q|)/4}.

Thus we estimate
\mathcal{K}_{1s}
using the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality as

(3.7)

\left|\langle w_{m}^{2}\mathcal{K}_{1s}(h_{1}),h_{2}\rangle\right|\leq t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}dq\penalty\ \int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}dp\penalty\ w_{m}^{2}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)\penalty\ \left|k_{1s}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p,t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)\right|\left|h_{1}(q)h_{2}(p)\right|\\
\leq t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}dq\penalty\ \left|h_{1}(q)\right|^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}dp\penalty\ w_{m}^{2}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)\penalty\ \left|k_{1s}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p,t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)\right|\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
\times\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}dp\penalty\ \left|h_{2}(p)\right|^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}dq\penalty\ w_{m}^{2}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)\penalty\ \left|k_{1s}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p,t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)\right|\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
\lesssim t^{-3\mathfrak{q}}e^{-R_{1}/4}\|h_{1}\|_{L^{2}_{p}}\|h_{2}\|_{L^{2}_{p}}.

By choosing
R_{1}>0
sufficiently large, this gives the desired estimate for
\mathcal{K}_{1s}
.

For
\mathcal{K}_{1c}
, from (
3.4
) and (
3.5
), there is a large constant such that

\displaystyle w_{m}^{2}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)\left|k_{1c}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p,t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)\right|\lesssim e^{-t^{2\mathfrak{q}}(|p|+|q|)/2}\penalty\ {\bf 1}_{t^{2\mathfrak{q}}|p|\leq R}\penalty\ {\bf 1}_{t^{2\mathfrak{q}}|q|\leq R}.

Now in addition, for some
0<\zeta<1
, we consider the sets

(3.8)

\displaystyle A\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}\big\{|p|+|q|\geq t^{-(1+\zeta)\mathfrak{q}}\big\},\qquad B\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}A^{c}=\big\{|p|+|q|<t^{-(1+\zeta)\mathfrak{q}}\big\},

and then split

\displaystyle k_{1c}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p,t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)

\displaystyle=k_{1c}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p,t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q){\bf 1}_{A}+k_{1c}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p,t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q){\bf 1}_{B}

\displaystyle\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}k_{1cA}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p,t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)+k_{1cB}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p,t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q),

and we similarly decompose

\mathcal{K}_{1c}=\mathcal{K}_{1cA}+\mathcal{K}_{1cB}.

Then we have

\displaystyle w_{m}^{2}k_{1cA}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p,t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)\lesssim e^{-t^{(1-\zeta)\mathfrak{q}}/4}e^{-t^{2\mathfrak{q}}(|p|+|q|)/4}\penalty\ {\bf 1}_{t^{2\mathfrak{q}}|p|\leq R}\penalty\ {\bf 1}_{t^{2\mathfrak{q}}|q|\leq R}.

Thus for
\mathcal{K}_{1cA}
with kernel
k_{1cA}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p,t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)
, using Cauchy–Schwarz as in (
3.7
),

\displaystyle|\langle w_{m}^{2}\mathcal{K}_{1cA}h_{1},h_{2}\rangle|\lesssim t^{-3\mathfrak{q}}e^{-t^{(1-\zeta)\mathfrak{q}}/4}\|{\bf 1}_{\leq R}h_{1}\|_{L^{2}_{p}}\|{\bf 1}_{\leq R}h_{2}\|_{L^{2}_{p}}.

On
B
instead, we use that

\displaystyle\max\{|p|,|q|\}\leq|p|+|q|<t^{-(1+\zeta)\mathfrak{q}}.

Hence, for
k_{1cB}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p,t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)
we have

\displaystyle w_{m}^{2}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}dq\penalty\ k_{1cB}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p,t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)\lesssim{\bf 1}_{|p|\leq R}\int_{0}^{t^{-(1+\zeta)\mathfrak{q}}}\rho^{2}d\rho\lesssim{\bf 1}_{|p|\leq R}t^{-3\mathfrak{q}}t^{-3\zeta\mathfrak{q}}.

The same estimate holds for
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}dp\penalty\ w_{m}^{2}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)k_{1cB}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p,t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)
. Thus for
\mathcal{K}_{1cB}
with kernel
k_{1cB}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p,t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)
, similarly to (
3.7
), we obtain

\displaystyle|\langle w_{m}^{2}\mathcal{K}_{1cB}h_{1},h_{2}\rangle|\lesssim t^{-3\mathfrak{q}}t^{-3\zeta\mathfrak{q}}\|{\bf 1}_{\leq R}h_{1}\|_{L^{2}_{p}}\|{\bf 1}_{\leq R}h_{2}\|_{L^{2}_{p}}.

This establishes the desired estimate for
\mathcal{K}_{1c}
. Then
\mathcal{K}_{1c}
is a compact operator because
k_{1c}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p,t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)\in L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{p}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{q})
.

We now consider
\mathcal{K}_{2}
as in (
1.39
) and in (
3.3
). We first explain how to obtain the form (
3.3
) for the kernel. Due to the diagonal structure of the FLRW metric, we can directly follow the special relativistic calculation in
[
75
, Appendix, starting on page 588]
to obtain, for some fixed constant
c_{1}>0
, that we have

\displaystyle k_{2}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p,t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \frac{c_{1}s^{3/2}}{\varrho{|p|}{|q|}t^{2\mathfrak{q}}}\mathcal{U}_{1}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p,t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)\exp\left(-\mathcal{U}_{2}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p,t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)\right),

where

\mathcal{U}_{2}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p,t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}\frac{\sqrt{s}}{2\varrho}t^{2\mathfrak{q}}|p-q|=\frac{t^{2\mathfrak{q}}}{2}|p-q|,

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}_{1}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p,t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}

\displaystyle\penalty\ \frac{1}{\mathcal{U}_{2}}+\frac{t^{2\mathfrak{q}}}{2}\frac{{|p|}+{|q|}}{\mathcal{U}_{2}^{2}}+\frac{t^{2\mathfrak{q}}}{2}\frac{{|p|}+{|q|}}{\mathcal{U}_{2}^{3}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \frac{2}{t^{2\mathfrak{q}}}\left(\frac{1}{|p-q|}+\frac{{|p|}+{|q|}}{|p-q|^{2}}+\frac{2}{t^{2\mathfrak{q}}}\frac{{|p|}+{|q|}}{|p-q|^{3}}\right).

Notice from (
1.24
) that we have

(3.9)

\displaystyle\frac{s^{3/2}}{\varrho{|p|}{|q|}t^{2\mathfrak{q}}}=\frac{2\big(|p||q|-p\cdot q\big)}{{|p|}{|q|}}=\left|\frac{p}{|p|}-\frac{q}{|q|}\right|^{2}.

Using additionally (
3.9
),
we decompose

\displaystyle k_{2}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p,t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)=k_{21}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p,t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)+k_{22}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p,t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)+k_{23}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p,t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q),

where

\displaystyle k_{21}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p,t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)=

\displaystyle\penalty\ 2c_{1}\frac{\left|\frac{p}{|p|}-\frac{q}{|q|}\right|^{2}}{t^{2\mathfrak{q}}|p-q|}e^{-t^{2\mathfrak{q}}|p-q|/2},

(3.10)

\displaystyle k_{22}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p,t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)=

\displaystyle\penalty\ 2c_{1}\frac{t^{2\mathfrak{q}}\left(|p|+|q|\right)\left|\frac{p}{|p|}-\frac{q}{|q|}\right|^{2}}{\left(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}|p-q|\right)^{2}}e^{-t^{2\mathfrak{q}}|p-q|/2},

\displaystyle k_{23}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p,t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)=

\displaystyle\penalty\ 4c_{1}\frac{t^{2\mathfrak{q}}\left(|p|+|q|\right)\left|\frac{p}{|p|}-\frac{q}{|q|}\right|^{2}}{\left(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}|p-q|\right)^{3}}e^{-t^{2\mathfrak{q}}|p-q|/2}.

To simplify no tation, we suppress the dependence on
t^{2\mathfrak{q}}
in the rest of this proof. In particular, we write
k_{2}(p,q)=k_{2}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p,t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)
and similarly for
k_{21}
,
k_{22}
and
k_{23}
.

The following inequality shows that
k_{2}(p,q)
as in (
3.10
) is integrable. We write

\displaystyle\frac{p}{|p|}-\frac{q}{|q|}=\frac{p-q}{|p|}+q\left(\frac{1}{|p|}-\frac{1}{|q|}\right).

Thus, by the triangle inequality

\displaystyle\left|\frac{p}{|p|}-\frac{q}{|q|}\right|\leq\frac{|p-q|}{|p|}+|q|\left|\frac{1}{|p|}-\frac{1}{|q|}\right|.

As a result,

\displaystyle\left|p\right|\left|\frac{p}{|p|}-\frac{q}{|q|}\right|\leq\left|p-q\right|+\left|\left|q\right|-\left|p\right|\right|\leq 2\left|p-q\right|.

After using symmetry, we conclude that

(3.11)

\displaystyle\max\{|p|,|q|\}\left|\frac{p}{|p|}-\frac{q}{|q|}\right|\leq 2|p-q|.

From (
3.10
) and (
3.11
) there is a uniform constant such that

\displaystyle k_{21}(p,q)+k_{22}(p,q)\lesssim\frac{e^{-t^{2\mathfrak{q}}|p-q|/2}}{t^{2\mathfrak{q}}|p-q|},\qquad k_{23}(p,q)\lesssim\frac{e^{-t^{2\mathfrak{q}}|p-q|/2}}{(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}|p-q|)^{2}}.

Then (
3.12
) shows that
k_{2}(p,q)
as in (
3.10
) is integrable in
\mathbb{R}^{3}_{q}
uniformly in time for any fixed
p\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
. For the weight
w_{m}^{2}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)
in (
1.41
) with
m\geq 0
we use
|p|\leq|p-q|+|q|
to see that

\displaystyle w_{m}^{2}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)\lesssim w_{m}^{2}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)+w_{m}^{2}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}(p-q)).

We thus conclude that

(3.12)

\begin{split}w_{m}^{2}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)\left(k_{21}(p,q)+k_{22}(p,q)\right)&\lesssim\min\big\{w_{m}^{2}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p),w_{m}^{2}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)\big\}\frac{e^{-t^{2\mathfrak{q}}|p-q|/4}}{t^{2\mathfrak{q}}|p-q|},\\
w_{m}^{2}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)k_{23}(p,q)&\lesssim\min\big\{w_{m}^{2}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p),w_{m}^{2}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)\big\}\frac{e^{-t^{2\mathfrak{q}}|p-q|/4}}{(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}|p-q|)^{2}}.\end{split}

These will be our main estimates for the kernels
k_{2j}(p,q)
above.

To proceed, we will employ a splitting to cut out the singularity, analogous to
[
75
, Equation (3.1)]
and also to
[
79
]
. Fix a small
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
. We choose a smooth nonnegative cut-off function
\chi(r)
satisfying

(3.13)

\chi=\chi(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}|p-q|)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}0&{\rm if}\penalty\ \penalty\ t^{2\mathfrak{q}}|p-q|\geq 2\varepsilon,\\
1&{\rm if}\penalty\ \penalty\ t^{2\mathfrak{q}}|p-q|\leq\varepsilon.\end{array}\right.

Now with (
3.10
) and (
3.13
) for
j\in\{1,2,3\}
we define

(3.14)

\displaystyle k_{2j}^{1-\chi}(p,q)\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}\left(1-\chi\right)k_{2j}(p,q),\qquad k_{2j}^{\chi}(p,q)\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}\chi k_{2j}(p,q)=k_{2js1}(p,q),

and will use the analogous splitting

\mathcal{K}_{2j}\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}\mathcal{K}_{2j}^{1-\chi}+\mathcal{K}_{2j}^{\chi},\quad j\in\{1,2,3\}.

We also denote
\mathcal{K}_{2j}^{\chi}=\mathcal{K}_{2js1}
with kernel
k_{2js1}(p,q)
.

Next we consider
\mathcal{K}_{2js1}
. To prove the desired estimate for the terms
\mathcal{K}_{2js1}
, it suffices to prove for
j\in\{1,2,3\}
the following uniform bounds

(3.15)

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}dq\penalty\ k_{2js1}(p,q)\lesssim t^{-6\mathfrak{q}}\varepsilon,\quad\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}dp\penalty\ w_{m}^{2}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)k_{2js1}(p,q)\lesssim w_{m}^{2}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)t^{-6\mathfrak{q}}\varepsilon.

We will only prove (
3.15
) for the
dq
integral because, after using (
3.12
) with
m>0
, the other proof is identical.
From (
3.12
) with
m=0
we have

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}dq\penalty\ k_{2js1}(p,q)\lesssim t^{-6\mathfrak{q}}\int_{0}^{2\varepsilon}d\rho\penalty\ e^{-\rho/4}\rho^{b(j)}\lesssim t^{-6\mathfrak{q}}\varepsilon^{b(j)+1}.

Here
b(1)=b(2)=1
and
b(3)=0
as in (
3.12
). In particular the estimate (
3.15
) holds. Then proceeding as in (
3.7
) we have

\displaystyle\left|\langle w_{m}^{2}\mathcal{K}_{2js1}(h_{1}),h_{2}\rangle\right|\lesssim t^{-3\mathfrak{q}}\varepsilon\|w_{m}h_{1}\|_{L^{2}_{p}}\|w_{m}h_{2}\|_{L^{2}_{p}}.

This is the desired estimate for
\mathcal{K}_{2j}^{\chi}=\mathcal{K}_{2js1}
.

We next consider
\mathcal{K}_{2j}^{1-\chi}
with kernel
k_{2j}^{1-\chi}(p,q)
as in (
3.14
). For this term using (
3.5
) with a different
R_{2}>0
for
j\in\{1,2,3\}
we decompose

k_{2j}^{1-\chi}(p,q)=\phi_{R_{2}}k_{2j}^{1-\chi}(p,q)+(1-\phi_{R_{2}})k_{2j}^{1-\chi}(p,q)\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}k_{2jc}(p,q)+k_{2js2}(p,q).

We will first estimate
\mathcal{K}_{2jc}
with kernel
k_{2jc}
. From (
3.10
), (
3.12
) and (
3.13
), there is a uniform constant such that

\displaystyle w_{m}^{2}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)k_{2jc}(p,q)\lesssim\varepsilon^{-2}e^{-t^{2\mathfrak{q}}|p-q|/2}\penalty\ {\bf 1}_{|t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p|\leq R_{2}}\penalty\ {\bf 1}_{|t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q|\leq R_{2}}.

Now, similarly to (
3.8
), for any
0<\zeta<1
we consider the regions

\displaystyle A\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}\{|p-q|\geq t^{-(1+\zeta)\mathfrak{q}}\},\qquad B\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}A^{c}=\{|p-q|<t^{-(1+\zeta)\mathfrak{q}}\},

and then split

k_{2jc}=k_{2jc}{\bf 1}_{A}+k_{2jc}{\bf 1}_{B}\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}k_{2jcA}+k_{2jcB},

and we use the analogous decomposition

\mathcal{K}_{2jc}=\mathcal{K}_{2jcA}+\mathcal{K}_{2jcB},\qquad\text{for}\quad j\in\{1,2,3\}.

On the set
A
we have

\displaystyle w_{m}^{2}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)k_{2jcA}\lesssim\varepsilon^{-2}e^{-t^{(1-\zeta)\mathfrak{q}}/4}e^{-t^{2\mathfrak{q}}|p-q|/4}\penalty\ {\bf 1}_{t^{2\mathfrak{q}}|p|\leq R_{2}}\penalty\ {\bf 1}_{t^{2\mathfrak{q}}|q|\leq R_{2}}.

Thus for
\mathcal{K}_{2jcA}
with kernel
k_{2jcA}
, as in (
3.7
) we have

\displaystyle|\langle w_{m}^{2}\mathcal{K}_{2jcA}h_{1},h_{2}\rangle|\lesssim t^{-3\mathfrak{q}}e^{-t^{(1-\zeta)\mathfrak{q}}/4}\|{\bf 1}_{\leq R}h_{1}\|_{L^{2}_{p}}\|{\bf 1}_{\leq R}h_{2}\|_{L^{2}_{p}}.

On
B
instead, we estimate the contribution of
k_{2jcB}
by

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}dq\penalty\ w_{m}^{2}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)k_{2jcB}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p,t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)\lesssim{\bf 1}_{|p|\leq R}\int_{0}^{t^{-(1+\zeta)\mathfrak{q}}}\rho^{2}d\rho\lesssim{\bf 1}_{|p|\leq R}t^{-3\mathfrak{q}}t^{-3\zeta\mathfrak{q}}.

Thus for
\mathcal{K}_{2jcB}
with kernel
k_{2jcB}
, similarly to (
3.7
), we obtain

\displaystyle|\langle w_{m}^{2}\mathcal{K}_{2jcB}h_{1},h_{2}\rangle|\lesssim t^{-3\mathfrak{q}}t^{-3\zeta\mathfrak{q}}\|{\bf 1}_{\leq R}h_{1}\|_{L^{2}_{p}}\|{\bf 1}_{\leq R}h_{2}\|_{L^{2}_{p}}.

This establishes the desired estimate for
\mathcal{K}_{2jcB}
and again
\mathcal{K}_{2jcB}
is a compact operator because
k_{2jcB}\in L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{p}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{q})
.

It remains to estimate
\mathcal{K}_{2js2}
with kernel
k_{2js2}
. However from (
3.5
) we have
|t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p|+|t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q|>R_{2}/2
, thus either
|t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p|
or
|t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q|
is strictly larger than
R_{2}/4
. Suppose that either
|t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q|\leq R_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}
or
|t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p|\leq R_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}
. If for instance
|t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q|\leq R_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}
, then

\displaystyle 1=\frac{|t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p|+|t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q|}{|t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p|+|t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q|}\leq\frac{t^{2\mathfrak{q}}|p-q|+2|t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q|}{|t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p|+|t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q|}\lesssim\frac{t^{2\mathfrak{q}}|p-q|+2R_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}}{R_{2}}.

The same estimate holds if
|t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p|\leq R_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}
. We plug the bounds above into (
3.12
), for
\varepsilon>0
fixed and
R_{2}>0
large, to obtain

(3.16)

\begin{split}w_{m}^{2}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)\left(k_{21s2}+k_{22s2}\right)&\lesssim\min\big\{w_{m}^{2}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p),w_{m}^{2}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)\big\}\frac{e^{-t^{2\mathfrak{q}}|p-q|/8}}{R_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon},\\
w_{m}^{2}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)k_{23s2}&\lesssim\min\big\{w_{m}^{2}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p),w_{m}^{2}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)\big\}\frac{e^{-t^{2\mathfrak{q}}|p-q|/8}}{R_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon^{2}}.\end{split}

This is our main estimate when either
|t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q|\leq R_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}
or
|t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p|\leq R_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}
.

Now, alternatively we suppose that both
|t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p|>R_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}
and
|t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q|>R_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}
. Notice that we can instead express (
3.10
) using (
3.9
) as

\displaystyle k_{21}(p,q)=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \frac{2c_{1}}{t^{2\mathfrak{q}}|p-q|}\frac{t^{2\mathfrak{q}}s}{{|p|}t^{2\mathfrak{q}}{|q|}t^{2\mathfrak{q}}}e^{-t^{2\mathfrak{q}}|p-q|/2},

(3.17)

\displaystyle k_{22}(p,q)=

\displaystyle\penalty\ 2c_{1}\frac{t^{2\mathfrak{q}}s}{\left(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}|p-q|\right)^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{{|p|}}+\frac{1}{{|q|}}\right)\frac{1}{t^{2\mathfrak{q}}}e^{-t^{2\mathfrak{q}}|p-q|/2},

\displaystyle k_{23}(p,q)=

\displaystyle\penalty\ 4c_{1}\frac{t^{2\mathfrak{q}}s}{\left(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}|p-q|\right)^{3}}\left(\frac{1}{{|p|}}+\frac{1}{{|q|}}\right)\frac{1}{t^{2\mathfrak{q}}}e^{-t^{2\mathfrak{q}}|p-q|/2}.

By using (
1.26
) and (
3.13
) in the expressions (
3.17
) above, for
\varepsilon>0
fixed and
R_{2}>0
large, for
j\in\{1,2,3\}
we obtain

(3.18)

\displaystyle w_{m}^{2}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)k_{2js2}(p,q)\lesssim\min\big\{w_{m}^{2}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p),w_{m}^{2}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)\big\}R_{2}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon^{-1}{e^{-t^{2\mathfrak{q}}|p-q|/8}}.

Again proceeding as in (
3.7
), using (
3.16
) and (
3.18
), we have

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle{w_{m}^{2}\mathcal{K}_{2js2}(h_{1})},{h_{2}}\right\rangle\right|\lesssim t^{-3\mathfrak{q}}R_{2}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon^{-1}\|w_{m}h_{1}\|_{L^{2}_{p}}\|w_{m}h_{2}\|_{L^{2}_{p}}.

First choose
\varepsilon>0
small, then choose
R_{2}>0
large to complete the proof.
∎

3.2.
Coercivity of the linearised operator

Let us study the basic functional analytic properties of the linearised operator
\mathcal{L}
.

Proposition 3.3
.

The linear operator
\mathcal{L}
from (
1.21
) is self-adjoint, that is,
\langle\mathcal{L}f,h\rangle=\langle f,\mathcal{L}h\rangle
for all
f
,
h\in L^{2}_{p}
. Moreover
\mathcal{L}
is non-negative, in other words
\langle\mathcal{L}h,h\rangle\geq 0
for all
h\in L^{2}_{p}
. Moreover
\mathcal{L}h=0
if and only if
h={\bf P}h
for
{\bf P}
from (
2.3
).

The properties above are standard, and their proofs follow directly from
[
32
]
. We now derive the main coercivity estimate for the linearised problem.

Proposition 3.4

(Coercivity estimate)
.

There exists a uniform constant

\delta_{0}>0
such that for all
h\in L^{2}_{p}
, and for all
t\geq 1
,

(3.19)

t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\langle\mathcal{L}h,h\rangle\geq\delta_{0}\|{\bf\{I-P\}}h\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}.

Proof of Proposition
3.4
.

Suppose that the coercive estimate is false. Then there is a time
t\geq 1
and a sequence of functions
h^{n}(p)
satisfying that

{\bf P}h^{n}=0,

\nu_{0}\|h^{n}\|_{L^{2}_{p}}=1
,
and

t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\langle{\mathcal{L}}h^{n},h^{n}\rangle=\nu_{0}\|h^{n}\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}-t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\langle{\mathcal{K}}h^{n},h^{n}\rangle\leq\frac{1}{n}.

We recall that
\nu_{0}>0
is the uniform constant obtained earlier in Proposition
1.3
.
Since
\nu_{0}\|h^{n}\|_{L^{2}_{p}}=1
, then

\{h^{n}\}
is weakly compact in
{L^{2}_{p}}
with limit point
h^{0}
.
By weak lower-semi continuity
\nu_{0}\|h^{0}\|_{L^{2}_{p}}\leq 1
. Furthermore,

t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\langle{\mathcal{L}}h^{n},h^{n}\rangle=1-t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\langle{\mathcal{K}}h^{n},h^{n}\rangle.

From Proposition
3.1
, we can split
\mathcal{K}=\mathcal{K}_{c}+\mathcal{K}_{s}
where
t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\langle{\mathcal{K}_{s}}h^{n},h^{n}\rangle
can be chosen arbitrarily small and
\mathcal{K}_{c}
is a compact operator. More precisely, for any small
\eta>0
we can choose
\mathcal{K}_{s}
as in Proposition
3.1
so that we have

t^{3\mathfrak{q}}|\langle\mathcal{K}_{s}h^{n},h^{n}\rangle|\leq\eta\|h^{n}\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}=\eta.

Then the remaining part
\mathcal{K}_{c}
has an integrable kernel which makes it a compact operator. Thus in particular

\lim_{n\to\infty}\langle{\mathcal{K}}_{c}h^{n},h^{n}\rangle=\langle{\mathcal{K}}_{c}h^{0},h^{0}\rangle.

Now fix
\epsilon>0
. Then choose
\eta=\frac{\epsilon}{4}
. Then

t^{3\mathfrak{q}}|\langle\mathcal{K}_{s}h^{n},h^{n}\rangle|\leq\frac{\epsilon}{4},\quad t^{3\mathfrak{q}}|\langle\mathcal{K}_{s}h^{0},h^{0}\rangle|\leq\frac{\epsilon}{4}.

Then for the resulting
{\mathcal{K}}_{c}
from Proposition
3.1
(which depends upon
\eta>0
) we can choose
N\in\mathbb{N}
large enough so that

\displaystyle t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\left|\langle{\mathcal{K}}_{c}h^{n},h^{n}\rangle-\langle{\mathcal{K}}_{c}h^{0},h^{0}\rangle\right|<\frac{\epsilon}{2},\quad\forall n\geq N.

Thus

\displaystyle t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\left|\langle{\mathcal{K}}h^{n},h^{n}\rangle-\langle{\mathcal{K}}h^{0},h^{0}\rangle\right|<\frac{\epsilon}{2}+\frac{\epsilon}{4}+\frac{\epsilon}{4}=\epsilon,\quad\forall n\geq N.

Combining these statements, we conclude that

\lim_{n\to\infty}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\langle{\mathcal{K}}h^{n},h^{n}\rangle=t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\langle{\mathcal{K}}h^{0},h^{0}\rangle.

In particular, we have that

t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\langle{\mathcal{K}}h^{0},h^{0}\rangle=1
,
or equivalently

t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\langle{\mathcal{L}}h^{0},h^{0}\rangle=\nu_{0}\|h^{0}\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}-1.

Since
\mathcal{L}\geq 0
, we conclude that
\nu_{0}\|h^{0}\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}=1
. Thus
\mathcal{L}h^{0}=0
which implies
h^{0}={\bf P}h^{0}
. On the other hand,

h^{n}=\{{\bf I-P}\}h^{n}
so that weak convergence implies
h^{0}=\{{\bf I-P}\}h^{0}
.
Thus
h^{0}=0
, which is a contradiction to
\nu_{0}\|h^{0}\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}=1
.
∎

Remark 3.5
.

The proof of Proposition
3.4
shows by contradiction the existence of a constant
\delta_{0}>0
such that (
3.19
) is satisfied. Alternatively, one could attempt to provide a quantitative proof of the existence of
\delta_{0}>0
as in previous works in the non-expanding case
[
61
,
6
,
60
]
.

The proof above follows the strategy of
[
45
, Lemma 6.9]
for the relativistic Boltzmann equation on
\mathbb{T}^{3}_{x}
in the massive case. See also the analysis in
[
34
]
.

3.3.
Trilinear estimate for the nonlinear term

We now prove
L^{2}_{p}
-trilinear estimates for the non-linear term
\Gamma(f,f)
in (
1.22
).

Lemma 3.6
.

Let
m\geq 0
and recall the weight
w_{m}^{2}=1+|t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p|^{m}
. Then, for all
f
,
h
, and
\eta
, the following uniform estimate holds,

|\langle w_{m}^{2}\Gamma(f,h),\eta\rangle|\lesssim\big\|w_{m}f\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})}\big\|w_{m}h\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})}\big\|w_{m}\eta\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})}.

Proof of Lemma
3.6
.

From (
1.22
), we have

\displaystyle\langle w_{m}^{2}\Gamma(f,h),\eta\rangle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\penalty\ dp\penalty\ w_{m}^{2}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)\eta(p)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\penalty\ dq\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\penalty\ d\omega\penalty\ v_{\text{\o}}\penalty\ \sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)}f(p^{\prime})h(q^{\prime})

\displaystyle\penalty\ -t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\penalty\ dp\penalty\ w_{m}^{2}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)\eta(p)f(p)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\penalty\ dq\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\penalty\ d\omega\penalty\ v_{\text{\o}}\penalty\ \sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)}h(q)

\displaystyle\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}

\displaystyle\penalty\ \Gamma_{1}-\Gamma_{2}.

We will estimate separately
\Gamma_{1}
and
\Gamma_{2}
.

First, we have the following uniform estimate

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\penalty\ dq\sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)}\left|h(q)\right|\lesssim\big\|J^{\frac{1}{4}}h\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\penalty\ dq\sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim t^{-3\mathfrak{q}}\big\|J^{\frac{1}{4}}h\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})}.

Thus, we estimate
\Gamma_{2}
using the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality as

\displaystyle\left|\Gamma_{2}\right|\lesssim

\displaystyle\penalty\ t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\penalty\ dp\penalty\ w_{m}^{2}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)\left|\eta(p)f(p)\right|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\penalty\ dq\sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)}\left|h(q)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\penalty\ \big\|J^{\frac{1}{4}}h\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})}\big\|w_{m}f\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})}\big\|w_{m}\eta\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})}.

This is the desired estimate for
\Gamma_{2}
.

Next using (
1.29
), we have the uniform estimate

w_{m}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)\lesssim w_{m}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p^{\prime})+w_{m}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q^{\prime})\lesssim w_{m}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p^{\prime})w_{m}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q^{\prime}).

Next we estimate
\Gamma_{1}
using again the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality as

\displaystyle\left|\Gamma_{1}\right|\lesssim

\displaystyle\penalty\ t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\penalty\ dp\penalty\ w_{m}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)\left|\eta(p)\right|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\penalty\ dq\penalty\ v_{\text{\o}}\penalty\ \sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)}\left|w_{m}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)h(q^{\prime})f(p^{\prime})\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\penalty\ t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\big\|w_{m}\eta\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}}\mathcal{I}_{1},

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1}\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}

\displaystyle\penalty\ \left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\penalty\ dp\penalty\ \left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\penalty\ dq\penalty\ v_{\text{\o}}\penalty\ \sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)}\left|w_{m}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q^{\prime})h(q^{\prime})w_{m}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p^{\prime})f(p^{\prime})\right|\right)^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\penalty\ t^{-3\mathfrak{q}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\penalty\ dp\penalty\ \int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\penalty\ dq\penalty\ v_{\text{\o}}\penalty\ \left|w_{m}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q^{\prime})h(q^{\prime})w_{m}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p^{\prime})f(p^{\prime})\right|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

We use the change of variables
(p^{\prime},q^{\prime})\mapsto(p,q)
with Jacobian (
1.34
) to obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1}\lesssim

\displaystyle\penalty\ t^{-3\mathfrak{q}}\big\|w_{m}f\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})}\big\|w_{m}h\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})}.

Note that, as explained in
[
77
]
, one can equivalently write
\mathcal{I}_{1}
in the Glassey-Strauss representation of (
1.33
) and then use the change of variables
(p^{\prime},q^{\prime})\mapsto(p,q)
in (
1.34
). Afterwards we can switch back to the center of momentum representation shown in this proof. This justifies the use of (
1.34
). ∎

3.4.
Estimates for averaged quantities

Now we prove estimates for averaged quantities in the momentum variables against the basis functions in (
2.6
).

Lemma 3.7
.

For any suitably regular
f
, and any element of
\{t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)\}_{\ell=1}^{13}
in (
2.6
), we have the following uniform estimates

(3.20)

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle{\bf\{I-P\}}f,t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)\right\rangle\right|\lesssim

\displaystyle\penalty\ \big\|J^{\frac{1}{4}}{\bf\{I-P\}}f\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}},

and

(3.21)

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\mathcal{L}({\bf\{I-P\}}f),t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)\right\rangle\right|\lesssim

\displaystyle\penalty\ t^{-3\mathfrak{q}}\big\|J^{\frac{1}{8}}{\bf\{I-P\}}f\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}}.

Proof of Lemma
3.7
.

For any element of (
2.6
), since
t\geq 1
, we can always split

(3.22)

\displaystyle t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)=t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)^{-\frac{1}{4}}J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)^{\frac{1}{4}}\leq t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)^{-\frac{1}{4}}J(p)^{\frac{1}{4}}.

Then we can include the factor
J(p)^{\frac{1}{4}}
with
{\bf\{I-P\}}f
and the rest of the upper bound above is exponentially decaying and suitably time-normalised. Then for
\langle{\bf\{I-P\}}f,t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)\rangle
, using the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality, we obtain (
3.20
).

For the estimate of (
3.21
), we use the self-adjoint property of
\mathcal{L}
as

\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{L}({\bf\{I-P\}}f),t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)\rangle=\langle{\bf\{I-P\}}f,\mathcal{L}(t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p))\rangle.

Then from (
1.21
), (
1.40
), and (
1.39
), we have for
\nu_{0}>0
that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p))=t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathcal{L}(\mathfrak{e}_{\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p))=\nu_{0}\mathfrak{e}_{\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)-t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathcal{K}(\mathfrak{e}_{\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)).

Further
\mathcal{K}=\mathcal{K}_{2}-\mathcal{K}_{1}
from (
1.39
) has the form

\displaystyle t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathcal{K}_{1}(\mathfrak{e}_{\ell})=t^{6\mathfrak{q}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\penalty\ dq\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\penalty\ d\omega\penalty\ v_{\text{\o}}\penalty\ \sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)}\penalty\ \mathfrak{e}_{\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q),

and we split
\mathcal{K}_{2}=\mathcal{K}_{21}+\mathcal{K}_{22}
where

\displaystyle t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathcal{K}_{21}(\mathfrak{e}_{\ell})=

\displaystyle\penalty\ t^{6\mathfrak{q}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\penalty\ dq\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\penalty\ d\omega\penalty\ v_{\text{\o}}\penalty\ \sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)}\sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q^{\prime})}\penalty\ \mathfrak{e}_{\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p^{\prime}),

\displaystyle t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathcal{K}_{22}(\mathfrak{e}_{\ell})=

\displaystyle\penalty\ t^{6\mathfrak{q}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\penalty\ dq\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\penalty\ d\omega\penalty\ v_{\text{\o}}\penalty\ \sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)}\sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p^{\prime})}\penalty\ \mathfrak{e}_{\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q^{\prime}).

For simplicity we denote each term above as
\mathcal{K}_{*}(\mathfrak{e}_{\ell})=\mathcal{K}_{*}(\mathfrak{e}_{\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}\cdot))
.
By using the structure of the basis vectors (
2.6
) and the conservation laws (
1.29
), we have that each term
\mathcal{K}_{*}
is of the form

\displaystyle t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathcal{K}_{*}(\mathfrak{e}_{\ell})=t^{6\mathfrak{q}}J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)^{\frac{1}{4}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\penalty\ dq\penalty\ v_{\text{\o}}\penalty\ {\mathfrak{b}}_{*\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p,t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)\penalty\ J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)^{\frac{1}{4}},

where each
{\mathfrak{b}}_{*\ell}={\mathfrak{b}}_{*\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p,t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)
can be written as

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{b}}_{1\ell}=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\penalty\ d\omega\penalty\ \mathfrak{e}_{\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)^{\frac{1}{4}}J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)^{\frac{1}{4}},

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{b}}_{21\ell}=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\penalty\ d\omega\penalty\ \mathfrak{e}_{\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p^{\prime})J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p^{\prime})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)}J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)^{\frac{1}{4}}J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)^{\frac{1}{4}},

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{b}}_{22\ell}=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\penalty\ d\omega\penalty\ \mathfrak{e}_{\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q^{\prime})J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q^{\prime})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)}J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)^{\frac{1}{4}}J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)^{\frac{1}{4}}.

Then each
{\mathfrak{b}}_{*\ell}
is uniformly bounded by using, for example, (
1.29
).

In particular each term
t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathcal{K}_{j}(\mathfrak{e}_{\ell})
for
j\in\{1,2\}
is of the form

\displaystyle t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathcal{K}_{j}(\mathfrak{e}_{\ell})=\mathfrak{m}_{j}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)^{\frac{1}{4}}.

Here
\mathfrak{m}_{j}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)
is a smooth uniformly bounded function. We also denote for simplicity
\mathfrak{m}_{0}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)=\nu_{0}\mathfrak{e}_{\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)^{-\frac{1}{4}}
. Then
t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathcal{L}(\mathfrak{e}_{\ell})=\sum_{j=0}^{2}\mathfrak{m}_{j}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)^{\frac{1}{4}}
. By using again (
3.22
), we have

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle{\bf\{I-P\}}f,\mathfrak{m}_{j}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)^{\frac{1}{4}}\right\rangle\right|\lesssim

\displaystyle\penalty\ t^{-3\mathfrak{q}}\big\|J^{\frac{1}{8}}{\bf\{I-P\}}\nabla_{x}f\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}}.

Thus, collecting the previous calculations, we obtain the estimate (
3.21
).
∎

See also
[
45
, Lemma 6.6]
for a similar estimate in the massive case when
\mathfrak{q}=0
. We also prove estimates for the nonlinear term when averaged against a basis function.

Lemma 3.8
.

For any suitably regular functions
f
,
h
, and any element of
\{t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)\}_{\ell=1}^{13}
in (
2.6
), we have the following uniform estimates

\displaystyle\left|\langle\Gamma(f,h),t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)\rangle\right|\lesssim

\displaystyle\penalty\ \|J^{\frac{1}{8}}f\|_{L^{2}_{p}}\|J^{\frac{1}{8}}h\|_{L^{2}_{p}}.

Proof of Lemma
3.8
.

From (
1.22
) and (
2.6
), we must estimate

\displaystyle\langle\Gamma(f,h),t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)\rangle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ t^{6\mathfrak{q}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\penalty\ dp\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\penalty\ dq\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\penalty\ d\omega\penalty\ v_{\text{\o}}\penalty\ \sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)}\mathfrak{e}_{\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)f(p^{\prime})h(q^{\prime})

\displaystyle\penalty\ -t^{6\mathfrak{q}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\penalty\ dp\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\penalty\ dq\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\penalty\ d\omega\penalty\ v_{\text{\o}}\penalty\ \sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)}\mathfrak{e}_{\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)f(p)h(q).

After using the pre-post collision change of variables
(p,q)\mapsto(p^{\prime},q^{\prime})
with Jacobian (
1.34
) on the first term, we have

\displaystyle\langle\Gamma(f,h),t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)\rangle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ t^{6\mathfrak{q}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\penalty\ dp\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\penalty\ dq\penalty\ \mathfrak{m}_{\Gamma}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p,t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)^{\frac{1}{4}}J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)^{\frac{1}{4}}f(p)h(q),

where now with (
1.29
) we have

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}_{\Gamma}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p,t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)=

\displaystyle\penalty\ v_{\text{\o}}\penalty\ J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)^{\frac{1}{4}}J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)^{\frac{1}{4}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\penalty\ d\omega\penalty\ \mathfrak{e}_{\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p^{\prime})J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p^{\prime})^{-\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\penalty\ -v_{\text{\o}}\penalty\ J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)^{\frac{1}{4}}J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)^{\frac{1}{4}}\mathfrak{e}_{\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\penalty\ d\omega\penalty\ .

Then
\mathfrak{m}_{\Gamma}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p,t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)
is suitably time-normalised and uniformly bounded. Lemma
3.8
follows by using the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality.
∎

See also the proof of
[
45
, Lemma 6.7]
in the case of massive particles when
\mathfrak{q}=0
.

4.
Macroscopic estimates

In this section we will estimate the solutions of the macroscopic equations from (
2.11
), (
2.21
) and (
2.22
). In the course of these estimates, we will use that the zero mode is zero according to Corollary
2.4
. The estimates in this section hold for any
0\leq\mathfrak{q}\leq 1
. We refer also to the macroscopic estimates obtained in
[
26
]
and in
[
20
, Section 5.1]
for the nonrelativistic Boltzmann equation

Theorem 4.1

(Estimates for macroscopic quantities)
.

Consider
T>1
, and a suitably regular solution
f
of (
1.19
) on
[1,T]\times\mathbb{T}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{p}
. Then the macroscopic quatities defined by (
2.4
) satisfy the following uniform estimate

(4.1)

\displaystyle\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}/2}[\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B},\mathcal{C}]\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{T}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}/2}{\bf\{I-P\}}f\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}+\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}}f\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}+\|f_{1}\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{p}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}_{k}}\left(\int^{T}_{1}t^{5\mathfrak{q}}\left|\widehat{\mathfrak{h}}(t,k)\right|^{2}dt\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}d\Sigma(k),

where
\big\|[\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B},\mathcal{C}]\big\|\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}\big\|\mathcal{A}\big\|+\big\|\mathcal{B}\big\|+\big\|\mathcal{C}\big\|
, and the definition of
\widehat{\mathfrak{h}}(t,k)
is provided below in (
4.7
), (
4.8
) and (
4.9
) with (
2.6
).

Proof.

First, we take the Fourier transform in (
2.11
) and (
2.22
) to obtain

(4.2)

\displaystyle t^{-3\mathfrak{q}}\partial_{t}\left(t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{j}\right)+\frac{2}{\sqrt{3}}t^{-\mathfrak{q}}ik_{j}\widehat{\mathcal{C}}

\displaystyle=-t^{-3\mathfrak{q}}\partial_{t}\left(t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}_{j+13}\right)+\hat{\mathfrak{u}}_{j+13},\quad(1\leq j\leq 3),

(4.3)

\displaystyle t^{-\mathfrak{q}}\sqrt{\frac{7}{3}}ik_{j}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}-\sqrt{3}\widehat{\mathcal{C}}\right)

\displaystyle=-t^{-3\mathfrak{q}}\partial_{t}\left(t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}_{j+5}\right)+\hat{\mathfrak{u}}_{j+5},

(4.4)

\displaystyle t^{-\mathfrak{q}}\frac{i}{2\sqrt{5}}\left(k_{1}\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{1}-k_{3}\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{3}\right)

\displaystyle=-t^{-3\mathfrak{q}}\partial_{t}\left(t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}_{9}\right)+\widehat{\mathfrak{u}}_{9},

(4.5)

\displaystyle t^{-\mathfrak{q}}\frac{i}{6\sqrt{5}}\left(k\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{B}}-3k_{2}\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{2}\right)

\displaystyle=-t^{-3\mathfrak{q}}\partial_{t}\left(t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}_{10}\right)+\widehat{\mathfrak{u}}_{10},

(4.6)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{5}}t^{-\mathfrak{q}}\left(ik_{j}\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{l}+ik_{l}\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{j}\right)

\displaystyle=-t^{-3\mathfrak{q}}\partial_{t}\left(t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}_{l+j+9}\right)+\hat{\mathfrak{u}}_{l+j+9},\quad(l\neq j).

In particular using the orthonormal basis functions in (
2.6
) we define

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}_{\ell}\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}

\displaystyle\penalty\ \left\langle{{\bf\{I-P\}}\hat{f}},{t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)}\right\rangle,

(4.7)

\displaystyle\widehat{\Theta}_{\ell}\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}

\displaystyle\penalty\ \left\langle{{\bf\{I-P\}}\hat{f}},{t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\frac{p}{|p|}\mathfrak{e}_{\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)}\right\rangle,

\displaystyle\hat{\mathfrak{l}}_{\ell}\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}

\displaystyle\penalty\ -\left\langle{\mathcal{L}({\bf\{I-P\}}\hat{f})},{t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)}\right\rangle,

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathfrak{h}}_{\ell}\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}

\displaystyle\penalty\ \left\langle{\widehat{\Gamma}(\hat{f},\hat{f})},{t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{e}_{\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)}\right\rangle,

\displaystyle\hat{\mathfrak{u}}_{\ell}\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}

\displaystyle\penalty\ -t^{-\mathfrak{q}}ik\cdot\widehat{\Theta}_{\ell}+\hat{\mathfrak{l}}_{\ell}+\widehat{\mathfrak{h}}_{\ell},

where in the second to last term, from (
1.22
), we have

(4.8)

\displaystyle\widehat{\Gamma}(\hat{f},\hat{h})(k,p)=

\displaystyle\penalty\ t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\penalty\ dq\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\penalty\ d\omega\penalty\ v_{\text{\o}}\penalty\ \sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)}[\hat{f}(p^{\prime})*\hat{h}(q^{\prime})](k)

\displaystyle\penalty\ -t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\penalty\ dq\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\penalty\ d\omega\penalty\ v_{\text{\o}}\penalty\ \sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)}[\hat{f}(p)*\hat{h}(q)](k),

with

\displaystyle{[\hat{f}(p^{\prime})*\hat{h}(q^{\prime})]}(k)

\displaystyle\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}_{l}}\hat{f}(k-l,p^{\prime})\hat{h}(l,q^{\prime})\,d\Sigma(l),

\displaystyle{[\hat{f}(p)*\hat{h}(q)]}(k)

\displaystyle\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}_{l}}\hat{f}(k-l,p)\hat{h}(l,q)\,d\Sigma(l).

We will also use the notation

(4.9)

\displaystyle\big|\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}\big|=\sum_{\ell=1}^{13}\big|\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}_{\ell}\big|,\quad\big|\widehat{\Theta}\big|=\sum_{\ell=1}^{13}\big|\widehat{\Theta}_{\ell}\big|,\quad\big|\hat{\mathfrak{l}}\big|=\sum_{\ell=1}^{13}\big|\hat{\mathfrak{l}}_{\ell}\big|,\quad\big|\widehat{\mathfrak{h}}\big|=\sum_{\ell=1}^{13}\big|\widehat{\mathfrak{h}}_{\ell}\big|.

In the rest of these estimates we write
\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}_{\ell}
,
\widehat{\Theta}_{\ell}
,
\hat{\mathfrak{l}}_{\ell}
,
\widehat{\mathfrak{h}}_{\ell}
, or
\hat{\mathfrak{u}}_{\ell}
to denote an arbitrary term with index
\ell
when the value of the index is unimportant.

We take the Fourier transform of (
2.21
) to obtain the local conservation laws

(4.10)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\widehat{\mathcal{A}}+\frac{1}{2}t^{-\mathfrak{q}}ik\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{B}}+\frac{3\mathfrak{q}}{t}\widehat{\mathcal{A}}

\displaystyle=-t^{-\mathfrak{q}}ik\cdot\widehat{\Theta}_{1},

(4.11)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{j}+2t^{-\mathfrak{q}}ik_{j}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}-\frac{2}{\sqrt{3}}\widehat{\mathcal{C}}\right)+\frac{3\mathfrak{q}}{t}\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{j}

\displaystyle=-t^{-\mathfrak{q}}ik\cdot\widehat{\Theta}_{j+1},\quad(1\leq j\leq 3),

(4.12)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\widehat{\mathcal{C}}+\frac{1}{2\sqrt{3}}t^{-\mathfrak{q}}ik\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{B}}+\frac{3\mathfrak{q}}{t}\widehat{\mathcal{C}}

\displaystyle=-t^{-\mathfrak{q}}ik\cdot\widehat{\Theta}_{5}.

We will use (
4.2
)-(
4.6
) and (
4.10
)-(
4.12
) with (
4.7
) to prove our desired estimates.

First we will estimate
\widehat{\mathcal{C}}(t,k)
. We multiply (
4.2
) by
-t^{4\mathfrak{q}}ik_{j}\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{C}}}(t,k)
and sum on
j
to obtain

\displaystyle\frac{2}{\sqrt{3}}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}|k|^{2}\left|\widehat{\mathcal{C}}(t,k)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=it^{\mathfrak{q}}\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{C}}}(t,k)\partial_{t}\left(t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\widehat{\mathcal{B}}\cdot k+\sum_{j=1}^{3}k_{j}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}_{j+13}\right)

\displaystyle\quad-it^{4\mathfrak{q}}\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{C}}}(t,k)\sum_{j=1}^{3}k_{j}\hat{\mathfrak{u}}_{j+13}.

We rearrange the time derivative as

\frac{2}{\sqrt{3}}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}|k|^{2}\left|\widehat{\mathcal{C}}(t,k)\right|^{2}=i\frac{d}{dt}\left(\left(t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\widehat{\mathcal{B}}\cdot k+\sum_{j=1}^{3}k_{j}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}_{j+13}\right)t^{\mathfrak{q}}\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{C}}}(t,k)\right)\\
-i\left(\widehat{\mathcal{B}}\cdot k+\sum_{j=1}^{3}k_{j}\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}_{j+13}\right)t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\partial_{t}\left(t^{\mathfrak{q}}\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{C}}}(t,k)\right)-it^{4\mathfrak{q}}\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{C}}}(t,k)\sum_{j=1}^{3}k_{j}\hat{\mathfrak{u}}_{j+13}.

After dividing by
|k|^{2}\geq 1
and integrating in time we have

\frac{2}{\sqrt{3}}\int_{1}^{T}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}|\widehat{\mathcal{C}}(t,k)|^{2}dt=\left.\frac{i}{|k|^{2}}\left(\left(\widehat{\mathcal{B}}\cdot k+\sum_{j=1}^{3}k_{j}\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}_{j+13}\right)t^{4\mathfrak{q}}\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{C}}}(t,k)\right)\right|_{t=1}^{T}\\
-\frac{i}{|k|^{2}}\int_{1}^{T}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{B}}\cdot k+\sum_{j=1}^{3}k_{j}\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}_{j+13}\right)t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\partial_{t}\left(t^{\mathfrak{q}}\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{C}}}(t,k)\right)dt-\frac{i}{|k|^{2}}\int_{1}^{T}t^{4\mathfrak{q}}\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{C}}}(t,k)\sum_{j=1}^{3}k_{j}\hat{\mathfrak{u}}_{j+13}dt.

We will estimate each of the terms on the right.

From (
4.7
) and (
3.20
) we have

(4.13)

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}(t,k)\right|+\left|\widehat{\Theta}(t,k)\right|\lesssim\big\|{\bf\{I-P\}}\hat{f}(t,k)\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}}.

Thus, for
|k|\geq 1
and
T\geq 1
we have

(4.14)

\left|\frac{1}{|k|^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{3}T^{4\mathfrak{q}}k_{j}\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}_{j+13}(T)\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{C}}}(T)\right|\lesssim T^{4\mathfrak{q}}\big\|{\bf\{I-P\}}\hat{f}(T,k)\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}}\left|\widehat{\mathcal{C}}(T,k)\right|\\
\lesssim T^{4\mathfrak{q}}\|\hat{f}(T,k)\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}\lesssim T^{6\mathfrak{q}}\|\hat{f}(T,k)\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2},

and similarly

(4.15)

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{|k|^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{3}k_{j}\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}_{j+13}(1)\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{C}}}(1)\right|\lesssim\big\|\hat{f}_{1}(k)\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}.

The term

\left.\frac{i}{|k|^{2}}\left(\left(\widehat{\mathcal{B}}\cdot k\right)t^{4\mathfrak{q}}\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{C}}}(t,k)\right)\right|_{t=1}^{T}
also satisfies the estimates in (
4.14
) and (
4.15
) at the respective points
t=T
and
t=1
. Thus

\displaystyle\left|\left.\frac{1}{|k|^{2}}\left(\left(\widehat{\mathcal{B}}\cdot k+\sum_{j=1}^{3}k_{j}\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}_{j+13}\right)t^{4\mathfrak{q}}\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{C}}}(t,k)\right)\right|_{t=1}^{T}\right|\lesssim T^{6\mathfrak{q}}\|\hat{f}(T,k)\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}+\big\|\hat{f}_{1}(k)\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}.

Next, from (
4.12
) we have

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\left(t^{\mathfrak{q}}\widehat{\mathcal{C}}\right)+\frac{1}{2\sqrt{3}}ik\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{B}}+\frac{2\mathfrak{q}}{t}t^{\mathfrak{q}}\widehat{\mathcal{C}}=-ik\cdot\widehat{\Theta}_{5}.

Hence

(4.16)

\displaystyle\left|t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\partial_{t}\left(t^{\mathfrak{q}}\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{C}}}\right)\right|\lesssim t^{3\mathfrak{q}}|k|\left(\left|\widehat{\mathcal{B}}\right|+\left|\widehat{\Theta}\right|\right)+2\mathfrak{q}t^{4\mathfrak{q}-1}\left|\widehat{\mathcal{C}}\right|.

Thus for
\mathfrak{q}\leq 1
and for any small
\eta>0
we have

\left|\frac{1}{|k|^{2}}\int_{1}^{T}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{B}}\cdot k+\sum_{j=1}^{3}k_{j}\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}_{j+13}\right)t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\partial_{t}\left(t^{\mathfrak{q}}\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{C}}}(t,k)\right)dt\right|\\
\leq C_{\eta}\int_{1}^{T}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\big\|{\bf\{I-P\}}\hat{f}(t,k)\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}dt+C_{\eta}\int^{T}_{1}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\left|\widehat{{\mathcal{B}}}(t,k)\right|^{2}dt+\eta\int^{T}_{1}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\left|\widehat{\mathcal{C}}(t,k)\right|^{2}dt.

Now from (
3.21
) we obtain

(4.17)

\displaystyle\big|\widehat{\mathfrak{l}}\big|\lesssim t^{-3\mathfrak{q}}\big\|{\bf\{I-P\}}\hat{f}(t,k)\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}}.

With (
4.7
) and (
4.17
) and the previous estimates for any small
\eta>0
we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{|k|^{2}}\int_{1}^{T}t^{4\mathfrak{q}}\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{C}}}(t,k)\sum_{j=1}^{3}k_{j}\hat{\mathfrak{u}}_{j+13}dt\right|\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \int_{1}^{T}t^{4\mathfrak{q}}\left(t^{-\mathfrak{q}}\left|\widehat{\Theta}(t,k)\right|+\big|\widehat{\mathfrak{l}}\big|+\big|\widehat{\mathfrak{h}}\big|\right)\left|\widehat{{\mathcal{C}}}(t,k)\right|dt

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \eta\int^{T}_{1}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\left|\widehat{{\mathcal{C}}}(t,k)\right|^{2}dt+C_{\eta}\int_{1}^{T}t^{5\mathfrak{q}}\big|\widehat{\mathfrak{h}}\big|^{2}dt

\displaystyle\penalty\ +C_{\eta}\int_{1}^{T}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\big\|{\bf\{I-P\}}\hat{f}(t,k)\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}dt.

We collect these estimates for
\widehat{\mathcal{C}}(t,k)
, choosing
\eta>0
sufficiently small, to obtain

(4.18)

\int_{1}^{T}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}|\widehat{\mathcal{C}}(t,k)|^{2}dt\lesssim T^{6\mathfrak{q}}\|\hat{f}(T,k)\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}+\big\|\hat{f}_{1}(k)\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}+\int^{T}_{1}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\left|\widehat{{\mathcal{B}}}(t,k)\right|^{2}dt\\
+\int_{1}^{T}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\big\|{\bf\{I-P\}}\hat{f}(t,k)\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}dt+\int_{1}^{T}t^{5\mathfrak{q}}\big|\widehat{\mathfrak{h}}\big|^{2}dt.

This is our primary estimate for
\widehat{\mathcal{C}}(t,k)
.

Next we estimate
\widehat{\mathcal{A}}-\sqrt{3}\widehat{\mathcal{C}}
. Multiply (
4.3
) by
-t^{4\mathfrak{q}}ik_{j}\left(\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}}-\sqrt{3}\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{C}}}\right)(t,k)
and sum on
j
to obtain

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{7}{3}}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}|k|^{2}\left|\widehat{\mathcal{A}}(t,k)-\sqrt{3}\widehat{\mathcal{C}}(t,k)\right|^{2}=

\displaystyle\penalty\ it^{\mathfrak{q}}\left(\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}}-\sqrt{3}\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{C}}}\right)(t,k)\partial_{t}\left(t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\sum_{j=1}^{3}k_{j}\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}_{j+5}\right)

\displaystyle\penalty\ -it^{4\mathfrak{q}}\left(\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}}-\sqrt{3}\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{C}}}\right)(t,k)\sum_{j=1}^{3}k_{j}\hat{\mathfrak{u}}_{j+5}.

We rearrainge the time derivative to conclude that

\sqrt{\frac{7}{3}}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}|k|^{2}\left|\widehat{\mathcal{A}}(t,k)-\sqrt{3}\widehat{\mathcal{C}}(t,k)\right|^{2}=i\frac{d}{dt}\left(\left(t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\sum_{j=1}^{3}k_{j}\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}_{j+5}\right)t^{\mathfrak{q}}\left(\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}}-\sqrt{3}\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{C}}}\right)\right)\\
