-i\left(\sum_{j=1}^{3}k_{j}\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}_{j+5}\right)t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\partial_{t}\left(t^{\mathfrak{q}}\left(\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}}-\sqrt{3}\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{C}}}\right)\right)-it^{4\mathfrak{q}}\left(\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}}-\sqrt{3}\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{C}}}\right)\sum_{j=1}^{3}k_{j}\hat{\mathfrak{u}}_{j+5}.

Next divide by
|k|^{2}\geq 1
and then integrate in time to achieve

\sqrt{\frac{7}{3}}\int_{1}^{T}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\left|\widehat{\mathcal{A}}(t,k)-\sqrt{3}\widehat{\mathcal{C}}(t,k)\right|^{2}dt\\
=\left.\frac{i}{|k|^{2}}\left(\left(t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\sum_{j=1}^{3}k_{j}\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}_{j+5}\right)t^{\mathfrak{q}}\left(\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}}-\sqrt{3}\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{C}}}\right)(t,k)\right)\right|_{t=1}^{T}\\
-\frac{i}{|k|^{2}}\int_{1}^{T}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{3}k_{j}\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}_{j+5}\right)t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\partial_{t}\left(t^{\mathfrak{q}}\left(\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}}-\sqrt{3}\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{C}}}\right)(t,k)\right)dt\\
-\frac{i}{|k|^{2}}\int_{1}^{T}t^{4\mathfrak{q}}\left(\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}}-\sqrt{3}\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{C}}}\right)(t,k)\sum_{j=1}^{3}k_{j}\hat{\mathfrak{u}}_{j+5}dt.

We will estimate each of the terms on the right.

Then as in (
4.14
) and (
4.15
) we have

\displaystyle\left|\left.\frac{1}{|k|^{2}}\left(\left(t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\sum_{j=1}^{3}k_{j}\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}_{j+5}\right)t^{\mathfrak{q}}\left(\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}}-\sqrt{3}\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{C}}}\right)(t,k)\right)\right|_{t=1}^{T}\right|\lesssim T^{6\mathfrak{q}}\|\hat{f}(T,k)\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}+\big\|\hat{f}_{1}(k)\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}.

Next subtract
\sqrt{3}
times (
4.12
) from (
4.10
) to obtain

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}-\sqrt{3}\widehat{\mathcal{C}}\right)+\frac{3\mathfrak{q}}{t}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}-\sqrt{3}\widehat{\mathcal{C}}\right)

\displaystyle=-t^{-\mathfrak{q}}ik\cdot\left(\widehat{\Theta}_{1}-\sqrt{3}\widehat{\Theta}_{5}\right).

In particular

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\left(t^{\mathfrak{q}}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}-\sqrt{3}\widehat{\mathcal{C}}\right)\right)+\frac{2\mathfrak{q}}{t}t^{\mathfrak{q}}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}-\sqrt{3}\widehat{\mathcal{C}}\right)

\displaystyle=-ik\cdot\left(\widehat{\Theta}_{1}-\sqrt{3}\widehat{\Theta}_{5}\right).

Thus we have

\displaystyle\left|t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\partial_{t}\left(t^{\mathfrak{q}}\left(\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}}-\sqrt{3}\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{C}}}\right)(t,k)\right)\right|\lesssim t^{3\mathfrak{q}}|k|\left|\widehat{\Theta}\right|+2\mathfrak{q}t^{4\mathfrak{q}-1}\left|{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}}-\sqrt{3}{\widehat{\mathcal{C}}}\right|.

We conclude, again using
\mathfrak{q}\leq 1
, for any small
\eta>0
that

\left|\frac{1}{|k|^{2}}\int_{1}^{T}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{3}k_{j}\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}_{j+5}\right)t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\partial_{t}\left(t^{\mathfrak{q}}\left(\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}}-\sqrt{3}\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{C}}}\right)(t,k)\right)dt\right|\\
\leq C_{\eta}\int_{1}^{T}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\big\|{\bf\{I-P\}}\hat{f}(t,k)\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}dt+\eta\int^{T}_{1}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\left|{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}}-\sqrt{3}{\widehat{\mathcal{C}}}\right|^{2}(t,k)dt.

Also using (
4.7
) and (
4.17
) and the prior estimates for any small
\eta>0
we obtain

\left|\frac{1}{|k|^{2}}\int_{1}^{T}t^{4\mathfrak{q}}\left(\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}}-\sqrt{3}\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{C}}}\right)(t,k)\sum_{j=1}^{3}k_{j}\hat{\mathfrak{u}}_{j+5}dt\right|\\
\leq\int_{1}^{T}t^{4\mathfrak{q}}\left(t^{-\mathfrak{q}}\left|\widehat{\Theta}(t,k)\right|+\left|\widehat{\mathfrak{l}}\right|+\left|\widehat{\mathfrak{h}}\right|\right)\left|{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}}-\sqrt{3}{\widehat{\mathcal{C}}}\right|(t,k)dt\\
\leq C_{\eta}\int_{1}^{T}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\big\|{\bf\{I-P\}}\hat{f}(t,k)\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}dt+C_{\eta}\int_{1}^{T}t^{5\mathfrak{q}}\left|\widehat{\mathfrak{h}}\right|^{2}dt+\eta\int^{T}_{1}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\left|{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}}-\sqrt{3}{\widehat{\mathcal{C}}}\right|^{2}(t,k)dt.

We collect the estimates above choosing
\eta>0
sufficiently small to obtain

(4.19)

\int_{1}^{T}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\left|\left({\widehat{\mathcal{A}}}-\sqrt{3}{\widehat{\mathcal{C}}}\right)(t,k)\right|^{2}dt\lesssim T^{6\mathfrak{q}}\|\hat{f}(T,k)\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}+\big\|\hat{f}_{1}(k)\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}\\
+\int_{1}^{T}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\big\|{\bf\{I-P\}}\hat{f}(t,k)\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}dt+\int_{1}^{T}t^{5\mathfrak{q}}\left|\widehat{\mathfrak{h}}\right|^{2}dt.

This is our primary estimate for
{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}}-\sqrt{3}{\widehat{\mathcal{C}}}
.

Now we take a linear combination of (
4.18
) and (
4.19
) to obtain

(4.20)

\int^{T}_{1}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\left|\widehat{[\mathcal{A},\mathcal{C}]}(t,k)\right|^{2}dt\lesssim T^{6\mathfrak{q}}\|\hat{f}(T,k)\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}+\big\|\hat{f}_{1}(k)\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}+\int^{T}_{1}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\left|\widehat{{\mathcal{B}}}(t,k)\right|^{2}dt\\
+\int_{1}^{T}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\big\|{\bf\{I-P\}}\hat{f}(t,k)\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}dt+\int_{1}^{T}t^{5\mathfrak{q}}\left|\widehat{\mathfrak{h}}\right|^{2}dt.

This will be our primary estimate for
\widehat{[\mathcal{A},\mathcal{C}]}(t,k)
.

Lastly we will estimate
\widehat{\mathcal{B}}
. To this end we multiply (
4.6
) by
-ik_{j}t^{\mathfrak{q}}\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}_{l}
and sum over
l\neq j
, to obtain

(4.21)

-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l=1\\
l\neq j\end{subarray}}^{3}\left(ik_{j}\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{l}+ik_{l}\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{j}\right)ik_{j}\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}_{l}=(k_{j})^{2}\left(|\widehat{\mathcal{B}}(t,k)|^{2}-(\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{j})^{2}\right)+k_{j}\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}_{j}\left(k\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{B}}-k_{j}\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{j}\right)\\
=(k_{j})^{2}|\widehat{\mathcal{B}}(t,k)|^{2}+k_{j}\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}_{j}\left(\left(k\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{B}}\right)-2k_{j}\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{j}\right).

Next, by taking suitable linear combinations of (
4.4
) and (
4.5
) we observe that for any
1\leq j\leq 3
we have

(4.22)

\displaystyle t^{-\mathfrak{q}}\frac{i}{6\sqrt{5}}\left(k\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{B}}-3k_{j}\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{j}\right)

\displaystyle=-t^{-3\mathfrak{q}}\partial_{t}\left(t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}_{*j}\right)+\widehat{\mathfrak{u}}_{*j},

where
\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}_{*j}
is some linear combination of
\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}_{9}
and
\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}_{10}
that depends upon
j
, and
\widehat{\mathfrak{u}}_{*j}
is a similar linear combination of
\widehat{\mathfrak{u}}_{9}
and
\widehat{\mathfrak{u}}_{10}
that depends upon
j
. Next multiply (
4.22
) by
-ik_{j}t^{\mathfrak{q}}\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}_{j}
to obtain

\displaystyle\left(k\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{B}}-2k_{j}\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{j}\right)\frac{k_{j}\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}_{j}}{6\sqrt{5}}=\frac{(k_{j})^{2}|\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{j}|^{2}}{6\sqrt{5}}+t^{-2\mathfrak{q}}ik_{j}\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}_{j}\partial_{t}\left(t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}_{*j}\right)-ik_{j}t^{\mathfrak{q}}\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}_{j}\widehat{\mathfrak{u}}_{*j}.

Note that
|k^{T}\widehat{\mathcal{B}}(t,k)|^{2}=\sum_{j=1}^{3}(k_{j})^{2}|\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{j}|^{2}
.
We plug this into (
4.21
), use (
4.6
), and sum on
j
to obtain

|k|^{2}|\widehat{\mathcal{B}}(t,k)|^{2}+|k^{T}\widehat{\mathcal{B}}(t,k)|^{2}=-\sqrt{5}\sum_{j=1}^{3}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l=1\\
l\neq j\end{subarray}}^{3}\left(ik_{j}\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{l}+ik_{l}\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{j}\right)ik_{j}\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}_{l}\\
+6\sqrt{5}it^{-2\mathfrak{q}}\sum_{j=1}^{3}k_{j}\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}_{j}\partial_{t}\left(t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}_{*j}\right)-6\sqrt{5}it^{\mathfrak{q}}\sum_{j=1}^{3}k_{j}\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}_{j}\widehat{\mathfrak{u}}_{*j}\\
=-t^{-2\mathfrak{q}}\sqrt{5}\sum_{j=1}^{3}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l=1\\
l\neq j\end{subarray}}^{3}ik_{j}\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}_{l}\partial_{t}\left(t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}_{l+j+9}\right)+\sqrt{5}t^{\mathfrak{q}}\sum_{j=1}^{3}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l=1\\
l\neq j\end{subarray}}^{3}ik_{j}\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}_{l}\hat{\mathfrak{u}}_{l+j+9}\\
+6\sqrt{5}it^{-2\mathfrak{q}}\sum_{j=1}^{3}k_{j}\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}_{j}\partial_{t}\left(t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}_{*j}\right)-6\sqrt{5}it^{\mathfrak{q}}\sum_{j=1}^{3}k_{j}\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}_{j}\widehat{\mathfrak{u}}_{*j}.

We have shown that

\displaystyle|k|^{2}|\widehat{\mathcal{B}}(t,k)|^{2}+|k^{T}\widehat{\mathcal{B}}(t,k)|^{2}=t^{-2\mathfrak{q}}\sum_{l=1}^{3}k_{l}\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}_{l}\partial_{t}\left(t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}_{\ddagger l}\right)-t^{\mathfrak{q}}\sum_{l=1}^{3}k_{l}\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}_{l}\widehat{\mathfrak{u}}_{\ddagger l},

where
\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}_{\ddagger l}
is a linear combination of the
\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}_{l+j+9}
and the
\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}_{*l}
,
and
\widehat{\mathfrak{u}}_{\ddagger l}
is a similar linear combination of the
\widehat{\mathfrak{u}}_{l+j+9}
and the
\widehat{\mathfrak{u}}_{*l}
.

After multiplying by
t^{3\mathfrak{q}}
and dividing by
|k|^{2}
we thus have

t^{3\mathfrak{q}}|\widehat{\mathcal{B}}(t,k)|^{2}+t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\frac{|k^{T}\widehat{\mathcal{B}}(t,k)|^{2}}{|k|^{2}}=\frac{t^{\mathfrak{q}}}{|k|^{2}}\sum_{l=1}^{3}k_{l}\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}_{l}\partial_{t}\left(t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}_{\ddagger l}\right)-\frac{t^{4\mathfrak{q}}}{|k|^{2}}\sum_{l=1}^{3}k_{l}\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}_{l}\widehat{\mathfrak{u}}_{\ddagger l}\\
=\frac{d}{dt}\left(\frac{t^{4\mathfrak{q}}}{|k|^{2}}\sum_{l=1}^{3}k_{l}\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}_{l}\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}_{\ddagger l}\right)-\frac{1}{|k|^{2}}\sum_{l=1}^{3}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}k_{l}\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}_{\ddagger l}\partial_{t}\left(t^{\mathfrak{q}}\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}^{j}\right)-\frac{t^{4\mathfrak{q}}}{|k|^{2}}\sum_{l=1}^{3}k_{l}\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}_{l}\widehat{\mathfrak{u}}_{\ddagger l}.

Similar to the previous cases, after integrating in time over
[1,T]
, we obtain

\int_{1}^{T}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\left(|\widehat{\mathcal{B}}(t,k)|^{2}+\frac{|k^{T}\widehat{\mathcal{B}}(t,k)|^{2}}{|k|^{2}}\right)dt=\frac{T^{4\mathfrak{q}}}{|k|^{2}}\sum_{l=1}^{3}k_{l}\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}_{l}(T)\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}_{\ddagger l}(T)\\
-\frac{1}{|k|^{2}}\sum_{l=1}^{3}k_{l}\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}_{l}(1)\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}_{\ddagger l}(1)-\frac{1}{|k|^{2}}\sum_{l=1}^{3}k_{l}\int_{1}^{T}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}_{\ddagger l}\partial_{t}\left(t^{\mathfrak{q}}\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}_{l}\right)dt-\frac{1}{|k|^{2}}\sum_{l=1}^{3}k_{l}\int_{1}^{T}t^{4\mathfrak{q}}\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}_{l}\widehat{\mathfrak{u}}_{\ddagger l}dt.

We will estimate each of the terms on the right side.

As in (
4.14
) and (
4.15
), for
|k|\geq 1
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{T^{4\mathfrak{q}}}{|k|^{2}}\sum_{l=1}^{3}\left|k_{l}\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}_{l}(T)\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}_{\ddagger l}(T)\right|+\frac{1}{|k|^{2}}\sum_{l=1}^{3}\left|k_{l}\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}_{l}(1)\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}_{\ddagger l}(1)\right|\lesssim T^{6\mathfrak{q}}\|\hat{f}(T,k)\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}+\big\|\hat{f}_{1}(k)\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}.

Next from (
4.11
) we have

\displaystyle t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\partial_{t}\left(t^{\mathfrak{q}}{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}_{j}\right)+2t^{3\mathfrak{q}}ik_{j}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}-\frac{2}{\sqrt{3}}\widehat{\mathcal{C}}\right)+\frac{2\mathfrak{q}}{t}t^{4\mathfrak{q}}\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{j}

\displaystyle=-t^{3\mathfrak{q}}ik\cdot\widehat{\Theta}_{j+1},\quad(1\leq j\leq 3),

Hence for any
1\leq j\leq 3
we have

\displaystyle\left|t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\partial_{t}\left(t^{\mathfrak{q}}{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}_{j}\right)\right|\lesssim t^{3\mathfrak{q}}|k|\left(\left|\widehat{\Theta}\right|+\left|\widehat{[\mathcal{A},\mathcal{C}]}\right|\right)+2\mathfrak{q}t^{4\mathfrak{q}-1}\left|{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}\right|.

Thus again using (
4.13
) for any
\mathfrak{q}\leq 1
and for any small
\eta>0
we have

\left|\frac{1}{|k|^{2}}\sum_{l=1}^{3}k_{l}\int_{1}^{T}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}_{\ddagger l}\partial_{t}\left(t^{\mathfrak{q}}\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}_{l}\right)dt\right|\\
\leq C_{\eta}\int_{1}^{T}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\big\|{\bf\{I-P\}}\hat{f}(t,k)\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}dt+\eta\int^{T}_{1}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\left|\widehat{{\mathcal{B}}}(t,k)\right|^{2}dt+\eta\int^{T}_{1}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\left|\widehat{[\mathcal{A},\mathcal{C}]}(t,k)\right|^{2}dt.

Then using (
4.7
), (
4.13
), and (
4.17
) for any small
\eta>0
we have

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{|k|^{2}}\sum_{l=1}^{3}k_{l}\int_{1}^{T}t^{4\mathfrak{q}}\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}_{l}\widehat{\mathfrak{u}}_{\ddagger l}dt\right|\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \int_{1}^{T}t^{4\mathfrak{q}}\left(t^{-\mathfrak{q}}\left|\widehat{\Theta}(t,k)\right|+\left|\widehat{\mathfrak{l}}\right|+\left|\widehat{\mathfrak{h}}\right|\right)\left|\widehat{{\mathcal{B}}}(t,k)\right|dt

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \eta\int^{T}_{1}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\left|\widehat{{\mathcal{B}}}(t,k)\right|^{2}dt+C_{\eta}\int_{1}^{T}t^{5\mathfrak{q}}\left|\widehat{\mathfrak{h}}\right|^{2}dt

\displaystyle\penalty\ +C_{\eta}\int_{1}^{T}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\big\|{\bf\{I-P\}}\hat{f}(t,k)\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}dt.

We collect all of these estimates for
\widehat{\mathcal{B}}(t,k)
to conclude for any small
\eta>0
that

(4.23)

\int_{1}^{T}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}|\widehat{\mathcal{B}}(t,k)|^{2}dt\lesssim T^{6\mathfrak{q}}\|\hat{f}(T,k)\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}+\big\|\hat{f}_{1}(k)\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}+\eta\int^{T}_{1}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\left|\widehat{[\mathcal{A},\mathcal{C}]}(t,k)\right|^{2}dt\\
+C_{\eta}\int_{1}^{T}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\big\|{\bf\{I-P\}}\hat{f}(t,k)\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}dt+C_{\eta}\int_{1}^{T}t^{5\mathfrak{q}}\big|\widehat{\mathfrak{h}}\big|^{2}dt.

This is our primary estimate for
\widehat{\mathcal{B}}(t,k)
.

To conclude the proof we multiply (
4.20
) by a suitably small constant
\eta_{1}>0
and then we add this to (
4.23
) choosing
\eta>0
in (
4.23
) small enough that
\eta-\eta_{1}>0
. Combining (
4.20
) with (
4.23
) we obtain

\int^{T}_{1}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\left|\widehat{[\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B},\mathcal{C}]}(t,k)\right|^{2}dt\lesssim T^{6\mathfrak{q}}\|\hat{f}(T,k)\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}+\big\|\hat{f}_{1}(k)\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}\\
+\int_{1}^{T}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\big\|{\bf\{I-P\}}\hat{f}(t,k)\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}dt+\int_{1}^{T}t^{5\mathfrak{q}}\left|\widehat{\mathfrak{h}}\right|^{2}dt.

Next we take the square root in the above inequality and use

(4.24)

\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(A+B)\leq\sqrt{A^{2}+B^{2}}\leq A+B,

further take
\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}
for the term
t^{6\mathfrak{q}}\|\hat{f}(t,k)\|_{L^{2}_{p}}
,
and then integrate the resulting inequality with respect to
d\Sigma(k)
over
\mathbb{Z}^{3}
to obtain the proof of the theorem.
∎

Remark 4.2
.

In the proof of Theorem
4.1
above, we have used
\mathfrak{q}\leq 1
to control the term
t^{4\mathfrak{q}-1}
on the right hand side of (
4.16
).

5.
Main energy estimate for
f

In this section we prove Proposition
5.1
, that is, our main energy estimate for the distribution function
f
. This relies on the coercivity estimate for the linearised operator
\mathcal{L}
in Proposition
3.4
, and the trilinear estimate for the nonlinearity in Lemma
3.6
.

Proposition 5.1

(Estimates for the distribution function)
.

Consider
T>1
, and a suitably regular solution
f
of (
1.19
) on
[1,T]\times\mathbb{T}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{p}
. Then the function
f
satisfies the following uniform estimate

(5.1)

\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}}f\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}+{\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}/2}{\bf\{I-P\}}f\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}\\
\lesssim\|{f}_{1}\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{p}}+{\|{t^{3\mathfrak{q}}f}\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}{\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}/2}f\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}.

The estimate above follows by suitably adapting the energy estimate in
[
20
, Section 3]
for the nonrelativistic Boltzmann equation on
\mathbb{T}^{3}_{x}
.

Proof.

We take the Fourier transform of (
1.19
) to obtain

(5.2)

\displaystyle\left(\partial_{t}+i\frac{p}{|t^{\mathfrak{q}}p|}\cdot k-\frac{2\mathfrak{q}}{t}p^{i}\partial_{p^{i}}\right)\hat{f}(t,k,p)+\mathcal{L}\hat{f}(t,k,p)=\widehat{\Gamma}(\hat{f},\hat{f})(t,k,p),

where
\hat{\Gamma}(\hat{f},\hat{h})(k,p)
is defined in (
4.8
). Take the product of (
5.2
) with the complex conjugate of
\hat{f}(t,k,p)
, denoted
\bar{\hat{f}}
, and then take the real part of the resulting equation to obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{d}{dt}-\frac{2\mathfrak{q}}{t}p^{i}\partial_{p^{i}}\right)|\hat{f}(t,k,p)|^{2}+\real{\bar{\hat{f}}\mathcal{L}\hat{f}}=\real{\bar{\hat{f}}\hat{\Gamma}(\hat{f},\hat{f})}.

Here
\real{z}
is the real part of a complex number
z
.
Integrating the above with respect to
p
we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{d}{dt}+\frac{6\mathfrak{q}}{t}\right)\|\hat{f}\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}(t,k)+\real{\left\langle{\mathcal{L}\hat{f}},{\hat{f}}\right\rangle}=\real{\left\langle{\hat{\Gamma}(\hat{f},\hat{f})},{\hat{f}}\right\rangle}.

From (
1.49
) this becomes

(5.3)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\left(t^{6\mathfrak{q}}\|\hat{f}\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}(t,k)\right)+t^{6\mathfrak{q}}\real{\left\langle{\mathcal{L}\hat{f}},{\hat{f}}\right\rangle}=t^{6\mathfrak{q}}\real{\left\langle{\hat{\Gamma}(\hat{f},\hat{f})},{\hat{f}}\right\rangle}.

Then integrating in time on the interval
[1,t]
, we have

(5.4)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}t^{6\mathfrak{q}}\|\hat{f}\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}(t,k)+\int^{t}_{1}\tau^{6\mathfrak{q}}\real{\left\langle{\mathcal{L}\hat{f}},{\hat{f}}\right\rangle}d\tau=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \frac{1}{2}\|\hat{f}_{1}(k,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}

\displaystyle\penalty\ +\int^{t}_{1}\tau^{6\mathfrak{q}}\real{\left\langle{\hat{\Gamma}(\hat{f},\hat{f})},{\hat{f}}\right\rangle}d\tau.

By the coercivity estimate of
\mathcal{L}
in Proposition
3.4
, there is
\delta_{0}>0
such that

\delta_{0}\|{\bf\{I-P\}}\hat{f}\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}(t,k)\leq t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\real{\left\langle{\mathcal{L}\hat{f}},{\hat{f}}\right\rangle}(t,k).

Thus it follows from (
5.4
) that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}t^{6\mathfrak{q}}\|\hat{f}\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}(t,k)+\delta_{0}\int^{t}_{1}\tau^{3\mathfrak{q}}\|{\bf\{I-P\}}\hat{f}\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}(\tau,k)d\tau\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \frac{1}{2}\|\hat{f}_{1}\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}(k)

\displaystyle\penalty\ +\int^{t}_{1}\tau^{6\mathfrak{q}}\real{\left\langle{\hat{\Gamma}(\hat{f},\hat{f})},{\hat{f}}\right\rangle}d\tau.

Take the square root on both sides, use (
4.24
), and then we further have

\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\|\hat{f}\|_{L^{2}_{p}}(t,k)+\sqrt{\delta_{0}}\left(\int^{t}_{1}\tau^{3\mathfrak{q}}\|{\bf\{I-P\}}\hat{f}\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}(\tau,k)d\tau\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{1}{2}\|\hat{f}_{1}\|_{L^{2}_{p}}(k)\\
+\sqrt{2}\left(\int^{t}_{1}\tau^{6\mathfrak{q}}\left|\real{\left\langle{\hat{\Gamma}(\hat{f},\hat{f})},{\hat{f}}\right\rangle}\right|d\tau\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

We have derived, for all
0\leq t\leq T
and any
k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}
, that

\displaystyle t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\|\hat{f}\|_{L^{2}_{p}}(t,k)

\displaystyle+\left(\int^{t}_{1}\tau^{3\mathfrak{q}}\|{\bf\{I-P\}}\hat{f}\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}(\tau,k)d\tau\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

(5.5)

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\left\{\|\hat{f}_{1}\|_{L^{2}_{p}}(k)+\left(\int^{t}_{1}\tau^{6\mathfrak{q}}\left|{\left\langle{\hat{\Gamma}(\hat{f},\hat{f})},{\hat{f}}\right\rangle}\right|d\tau\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\},

with
C_{0}=\max\{2,\sqrt{2/\delta_{0}}\}>0
. By the following well-known identity,

\displaystyle\langle\Gamma(f,f),h\rangle=\langle\Gamma(f,f),{\bf\{I-P\}}h\rangle,

it follows directly that

\left\langle{\hat{\Gamma}(\hat{f},\hat{f})},{\hat{f}}\right\rangle=\left\langle{\hat{\Gamma}(\hat{f},\hat{f})},{{\bf\{I-P\}}\hat{f}}\right\rangle.

Then, taking
\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}
on both sides of (
5.5
) and integrating the resulting inequality with respect to
d\Sigma(k)
over
\mathbb{Z}^{3}
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left(t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\|\hat{f}(t,k)\|_{L^{2}_{p}}\right)d\Sigma(k)+\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\left(\int^{T}_{1}\tau^{3\mathfrak{q}}\|{\bf\{I-P\}}f(\tau,k)\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}d\tau\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}d\Sigma(k)

(5.6)

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\left\{\|\hat{f}_{1}\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{p}}+\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\left(\int^{T}_{1}\tau^{6\mathfrak{q}}\left|{\left\langle{\hat{\Gamma}(\hat{f},\hat{f})},{{\bf\{I-P\}}\hat{f}}\right\rangle}\right|d\tau\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}d\Sigma(k)\right\}.

We can estimate the last term on the right-hand side of (
5.6
) as

(5.7)

\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\left(\int^{T}_{1}t^{6\mathfrak{q}}\left|\left\langle{\hat{\Gamma}(\hat{f},\hat{f})},{{{\bf\{I-P\}}\hat{f}}}\right\rangle\right|dt\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}d\Sigma(k)\\
\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\left(\int^{T}_{1}t^{6\mathfrak{q}}\big\|{{\bf\{I-P\}}\hat{f}}(k)\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}}\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\big\|\hat{f}(k-l)\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}}\big\|\hat{f}(l)\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}}d\Sigma(l)dt\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}d\Sigma(k).

Then (
5.7
) is a direct consequence of Lemma
5.2
that is stated and proven below.

We now estimate the upper bound in (
5.7
). Applying the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality to
\int_{1}^{T}(\cdot)dt
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}_{k}}\left(\int^{T}_{1}t^{6\mathfrak{q}}\big\|{\bf\{I-P\}}\hat{f}(t,k)\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}}\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}_{l}}\|\hat{f}(t,k-l)\|_{L^{2}_{p}}\big\|\hat{f}(t,l)\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}}d\Sigma(l)dt\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}d\Sigma(k)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}_{k}}\left(\int^{T}_{1}t^{6\mathfrak{q}}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}_{l}}\|\hat{f}(t,k-l)\|_{L^{2}_{p}}\big\|\hat{f}(t,l)\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}}d\Sigma(l)\right)^{2}dt\right)^{1/4}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\left(\int^{T}_{1}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\big\|{\bf\{I-P\}}\hat{f}(t,k)\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}dt\right)^{1/4}d\Sigma(k),

and then use Young’s inequality with small constant
\eta>0
to observe that

(5.8)

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}\leq\eta\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}_{k}}\left(\int^{T}_{1}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\big\|{\bf\{I-P\}}\hat{f}(t,k)\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}dt\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}d\Sigma(k)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{4\eta}\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}_{k}}\left(\int^{T}_{1}t^{6\mathfrak{q}}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}_{l}}\|\hat{f}(t,k-l)\|_{L^{2}_{p}}\big\|\hat{f}(t,l)\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}}d\Sigma(l)\right)^{2}dt\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}d\Sigma(k).

We now recall the following version of Minkowski’s inequality

(5.9)

\big\|\|\cdot\|_{L^{1}_{l}}\big\|_{L^{2}_{t}}\leq\big\|\|\cdot\|_{L^{2}_{t}}\big\|_{L^{1}_{l}}.

For the second term on the right-hand side of (
5.8
),
we use (
5.9
) to obtain

\left(\int^{T}_{1}t^{6\mathfrak{q}}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}_{l}}\|\hat{f}(t,k-l)\|_{L^{2}_{p}}\big\|\hat{f}(t,l)\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}}d\Sigma(l)\right)^{2}dt\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
\leq\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}_{l}}\left(\int^{T}_{1}t^{6\mathfrak{q}}\|\hat{f}(t,k-l)\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\big\|\hat{f}(t,l)\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}dt\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}d\Sigma(l)\\
\leq\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}_{k}}\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}_{l}}\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq T}\left(\tau^{3\mathfrak{q}}\|\hat{f}(\tau,k-l)\|_{L^{2}_{p}}\right)\left(\int^{T}_{1}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\big\|\hat{f}(t,l)\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}dt\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}d\Sigma(l)d\Sigma(k).

Then Fubini’s theorem and translation invariance imply that

\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}_{k}}\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}_{l}}\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq T}\left(\tau^{3\mathfrak{q}}\|\hat{f}(\tau,k-l)\|_{L^{2}_{p}}\right)\left(\int^{T}_{1}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\big\|\hat{f}(t,l)\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}dt\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}d\Sigma(l)d\Sigma(k)\\
=\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}_{l}}d\Sigma(l)\left(\int^{T}_{1}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\big\|\hat{f}(t,l)\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}dt\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}_{k}}d\Sigma(k)\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq T}\left(\tau^{3\mathfrak{q}}\|\hat{f}(\tau,k-l)\|_{L^{2}_{p}}\right)\\
=\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}}{f}\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}/2}{f}\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}.

Applying the estimates above to the second term on the right side of (
5.8
) and further using (
5.6
) and (
5.7
) leads to the desired estimate (
5.1
) in the statement of the proposition.
∎

We now state and prove the following lemma.

Lemma 5.2
.

For all
f
,
h
, and
\eta
, the following uniform estimate holds,

(5.10)

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle{\widehat{\Gamma}(\hat{f},\hat{h})(k)},{\hat{\eta}(k)}\right\rangle\right|\lesssim\big\|\hat{\eta}(k)\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}}\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\|\hat{f}(k-l)\|_{L^{2}_{p}}\big\|\hat{h}(l)\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}}\,d\Sigma(l).

Notice that (
5.10
) directly implies (
5.7
).

Proof.

By the definition (
4.8
) of
\hat{\Gamma}(\cdot,\cdot)
and Fubini’s theorem, we obtain

(5.11)

\displaystyle\left\langle{\hat{\Gamma}(\hat{f},\hat{h})(k)},{\hat{\eta}(k)}\right\rangle=\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\left\langle{\Gamma(\hat{f}(k-l),\hat{h}(l))},{\hat{\eta}(k)}\right\rangle d\Sigma(l).

This calculation is performed in detail in
[
20
, Lemma 3.2]
. In our case, we have

\left\langle{\hat{\Gamma}(\hat{f},\hat{h})(k)},{\hat{\eta}(k)}\right\rangle\\
=t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}dp\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}dq\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}d\omega\penalty\ v_{\text{\o}}\sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)}\left\{[\hat{f}(p^{\prime})*\hat{h}(q^{\prime})](k)-[\hat{f}(p)*\hat{h}(q)](k)\right\}\bar{\hat{\eta}}(p,k)\\
=t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\penalty\ dp\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\penalty\ dq\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\penalty\ d\omega\penalty\ v_{\text{\o}}\penalty\ \sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)}\\
\times\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\left\{\hat{f}(k-l,p^{\prime})\hat{h}(l,q^{\prime})-\hat{f}(k-l,p)\hat{h}(l,q)\right\}\bar{\hat{\eta}}(p,k)d\Sigma(l)\\
=\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\left\langle{\Gamma(\hat{f}(k-l),\hat{h}(l))},{\hat{\eta}(k)}\right\rangle d\Sigma(l).

We conclude

(5.12)

\left|\left\langle{\hat{\Gamma}(\hat{f},\hat{h})(k)},{\hat{\eta}(k)}\right\rangle\right|\leq\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\left|\left\langle{\Gamma(\hat{f}(k-l),\hat{h}(l))},{\hat{\eta}(k)}\right\rangle\right|d\Sigma(l).

Then, by Lemma
3.6
we have

(5.13)

\left|\left\langle{\Gamma(\hat{f}(k-l),\hat{h}(l))},{\hat{\eta}(k)}\right\rangle\right|\lesssim\|\hat{f}(k-l)\|_{L^{2}_{p}}\big\|\hat{h}(l)\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}}\big\|\hat{\eta}(k)\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}}.

With (
5.13
) above, the desired estimate (
5.10
) follows from (
5.12
). ∎

6.
Global stability of the Maxwell–Jüttner equilibrium

In this section, we will prove Theorem
1.4
. In Section
6.1
, we first show the local well-posedness result Theorem
1.4
is based on. In Section
6.2
, we show global existence and the uniform decay estimate (
6.4
) in Theorem
1.4
. In Section
6.3
, we prove the faster decay estimates in the case when
\mathfrak{q}\in(0,1/3]
. In Section
6.4
, we prove exponential decay in the nonexpanding case
\mathfrak{q}=0
. In Section
6.5
, we prove time-decay for weighted energy norms. Finally, in Section
6.6
, we prove propagation of spatial regularity.

6.1.
Local existence and uniqueness around the equilibrium

We prove here local existence and uniqueness of solutions for the massless Boltzmann equation on FLRW spacetime (
1.19
) around the Maxwell–Jüttner equilibrium.

Theorem 6.1

(Local existence and uniqueness around the equilibrium)
.

Let
\mathfrak{q}\in[0,1]
. Under the assumptions of Theorem
1.4
, there is a small
\epsilon_{0}>0
, a
T_{0}=T_{0}(\epsilon_{0})>0
and some
C_{0}=C_{0}(T_{0})>0
such that if

(6.1)

\|f_{1}\|_{L_{k}^{1}L^{2}_{p}}\leq\epsilon_{0},

then the massless Boltzmann equation on FLRW spacetime around the equilibrium, (
1.19
),
admits a unique local-in-time solution

f(t,x,p),\penalty\ \penalty\ \ 0\leq t\leq T_{0}<\infty,\penalty\ \penalty\ \ x\in\mathbb{T}^{3},\penalty\ \penalty\ \ p\in\mathbb{R}^{3},

which satisfies the uniform estimate

(6.2)

\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}}f\|_{L^{1}_{{k}}L^{\infty}_{T_{0}}L^{2}_{p}}+\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}/2}f\|_{L^{1}_{{k}}L^{2}_{T_{0}}L^{2}_{p}}\leq C_{0}\|f_{1}\|_{L_{{k}}^{1}L^{2}_{p}}.

If additionally

F_{1}(x,p)=J(p)+J^{\frac{1}{2}}(p)f_{1}(x,p)\geq 0
,
then the solution satisfies

F(t,{x},p)=J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)+\sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)}f(t,{x},p)\geq 0.

To prove Theorem
6.1
, we start from the following linear inhomogeneous problem

(6.3)

\begin{split}\partial_{t}h+\frac{p^{i}}{|t^{\mathfrak{q}}p|}\partial_{x^{i}}h-\frac{2\mathfrak{q}}{t}p^{i}\partial_{p^{i}}h+t^{-3\mathfrak{q}}\nu_{0}h-\Gamma(h,\mathbbm{W})=&\penalty\ \mathcal{K}(\mathbbm{W}),\\
h(t=1,x,p)=&\penalty\ h_{1}(x,p),\end{split}

where
\mathbbm{W}=\mathbbm{W}(t,x,p)
is a given function. The solvability of (
6.3
) is guaranteed by the following lemma.

Lemma 6.2
.

Let
h_{1}\in L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{p}
be an initial datum. We suppose that for some
\epsilon_{0}>0
,
T_{0}>0
and
T\in(0,T_{0}]
the given function
{\mathbbm{W}}
satisfies

(6.4)

\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}}{\mathbbm{W}}\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}+\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}/2}{\mathbbm{W}}\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}\leq\epsilon_{0}.

Then, for
\epsilon_{0}>0
in (
6.4
) small enough, there exists
T_{0}>0
and
C_{1}=C_{1}(T_{0})>0
such that for any
T\in(0,T_{0}]
the initial value problem (
6.3
) admits a unique weak solution
h(t,x,p)
for
0\leq t\leq T
,
x\in\mathbb{T}^{3}
and
p\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
satisfying

\displaystyle\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}}h\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}+\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}/2}h\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}\leq C_{0}\left(\|h_{1}\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{p}}+\sqrt{T}\|{\mathbbm{W}}\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}\right).

Regarding the local-in-time existence and uniqueness of solutions to the linear equation (
6.3
), the proof of Lemma
6.2
is completely standard and omitted.

Proof of Theorem
6.1
.

We will first build a sequence of approximate solutions denoted by
(f^{n}(t,x,p))_{n=0}^{\infty}
for the problem
(
1.19
) with initial data (
1.20
). For
n=0,1,2,\ldots
, we use the following iterative scheme

\displaystyle\partial_{t}f^{n+1}+\frac{p^{i}}{|t^{\mathfrak{q}}p|}\partial_{x^{i}}f^{n+1}-\frac{2\mathfrak{q}}{t}p^{i}\partial_{p^{i}}f^{n+1}+t^{-3\mathfrak{q}}\nu_{0}f^{n+1}=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \Gamma(f^{n+1},f^{n})+\mathcal{K}(f^{n}),

(6.5)

\displaystyle f^{0}(t,x,p)=f_{1}(x,p),\qquad f^{n+1}(t=1,x,p)=

\displaystyle\penalty\ f_{1}(x,p).

Then, by using Lemma
6.2
, and the a priori estimates in Section
3
, it is a standard procedure to apply an induction argument to show that there are
\epsilon_{0}>0
and
T_{0}>0
such that if (
6.1
) holds then the approximation sequence
(f^{n}(t,x,p))_{n=0}^{\infty}
is well-defined in the Banach space
L^{1}_{k}L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}\cap L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{T}L^{2}_{p}
for any
1<T\leq T_{0}
.

We will prove that for a short time,
T>1
, the iterated solutions to (
6.5
) satisfy the following uniform estimate

(6.6)

\sup_{n\geq 0}\left(\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}}f^{n}\|_{L^{1}_{{k}}L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}+\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}/2}f^{n}\|_{L^{1}_{{k}}L^{2}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}\right)\leq 4C_{1}\|f_{1}\|_{L^{1}_{{k}}L^{2}_{p}},

where
C_{1}>0
is a uniform constant.

Fix an integer
m\geq 1
and suppose that for some small
T-1>0
, that
(
6.6
) holds for
all
n\in\{0,1,2,3,\ldots,m\}
for some constant
C_{1}>0
that is uniform in
m
. Then it is sufficient to prove that (
6.6
) holds for
n=m+1
by induction. We mention that (
6.6
) holds for
n=0
with
f^{0}=f_{1}
as defined in (
6.5
).

Then, for the iterative problem (
6.5
), following the proof of Proposition
5.1
,

\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}}f^{n+1}\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}+{\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}/2}f^{n+1}\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}\\
\leq C_{1}\|{f}_{1}\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{p}}+C_{2}{\|{t^{3\mathfrak{q}}f^{n+1}}\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}{\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}/2}f^{n}\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}\\
+C_{2}\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}_{k}}\left(\int^{T}_{1}\tau^{6\mathfrak{q}}\left|{\left\langle{\mathcal{K}(\hat{f}^{n})},{\hat{f}^{n+1}}\right\rangle}(\tau,k)\right|d\tau\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}d\Sigma(k).

Above
C_{2}>0
and the constant
C_{1}>0
in (
6.6
) is given by

C_{1}\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}2\max\big\{2,\sqrt{2/\nu_{0}}\big\}>0.

For the last term, from Proposition
3.1
, we have

\tau^{6\mathfrak{q}}\left|\left\langle{\mathcal{K}(\widehat{f}^{n})},{\widehat{f}^{n+1}}\right\rangle\right|\lesssim\tau^{3\mathfrak{q}}\big\|\widehat{f}^{n}\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}}(\tau)\big\|\widehat{f}^{n+1}\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}}(\tau).

We conclude that

\int^{T}_{1}\tau^{6\mathfrak{q}}\left|\left\langle{\mathcal{K}(\widehat{f}^{n})},{\widehat{f}^{n+1}}\right\rangle(\tau,k)\right|d\tau\lesssim(T-1)\big\|\widehat{f}^{n}(k)\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\widehat{f}^{n+1}(k)\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}.

Thus for any small
\delta>0
we have

\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}_{k}}\left(\int^{T}_{1}\tau^{6\mathfrak{q}}\left|{\left\langle{\mathcal{K}(\hat{f}^{n})},{\hat{f}^{n+1}}\right\rangle}(\tau,k)\right|d\tau\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}d\Sigma(k)\\
\leq C(T-1)^{\frac{1}{2}}\big\|{f}^{n}\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}}{f}^{n+1}\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
\leq C_{\delta}(T-1)\big\|{f}^{n}\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}+\delta\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}}{f}^{n+1}\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}.

Thus for
\delta>0
small enough, and some
C_{3}>0
, we have

\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}}f^{n+1}\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}+{\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}/2}f^{n+1}\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}\leq 2C_{1}\|{f}_{1}\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{p}}\\
+C_{3}{\|{t^{3\mathfrak{q}}f^{n+1}}\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}{\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}/2}f^{n}\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}+C_{3}(T-1)\big\|{f}^{n}\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}.

Then by the induction assumption, (
6.6
) for
n=m
, we have

\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}}f^{n+1}\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}+{\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}/2}f^{n+1}\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}\leq 2C_{1}\|{f}_{1}\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{p}}\\
+2C_{1}C_{3}{\|{t^{3\mathfrak{q}}f^{n+1}}\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}\|f_{1}\|_{L^{1}_{{k}}L^{2}_{p}}+2C_{1}C_{3}(T-1)\|f_{1}\|_{L^{1}_{{k}}L^{2}_{p}}.

Thus, we obtain

\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}}f^{n+1}\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}+{\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}/2}f^{n+1}\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}\leq C_{4}\|{f}_{1}\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{p}},

where

C_{4}=\frac{2C_{1}+2C_{1}C_{3}(T-1)}{1-2C_{1}C_{3}\|f_{1}\|_{L^{1}_{{k}}L^{2}_{p}}}.

Then for
T-1>0
and
\|f_{1}\|_{L^{1}_{{k}}L^{2}_{p}}
sufficiently small, we observe that
C_{4}\leq 4C_{1}
. This proves (
6.6
) by induction. By a similar standard procedure we can also show that
(f^{n}(t,x,p))_{n=0}^{\infty}
is a Cauchy sequence in this space. Then the limit function
f(t,x,p)
is a weak solution of (
1.19
) satisfying the estimate (
6.2
).

We now prove the uniqueness of the local-in-time solution. Suppose alternatively that we have two solutions
f
and
h
to (
1.19
), both satisfying the local-in-time estimate (
6.2
) for
1\leq t\leq T_{0}
with initial data
f_{1}
and
h_{1}
, respectively. Then the difference of the two solutions
f-h
satisfies

\left(\partial_{t}+\frac{p^{i}}{|t^{\mathfrak{q}}p|}\partial_{x^{i}}-\frac{2\mathfrak{q}}{t}p^{i}\partial_{p^{i}}\right)\left(f-h\right)+\mathcal{L}\left(f-h\right)=\Gamma(f,f)-\Gamma(h,h)\\
=\Gamma(f,f-h)+\Gamma(f-h,h).

We can again apply the same estimates as in the proof of Proposition
5.1
to obtain

(6.7)

\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}}(f-h)\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{\infty}_{T_{0}}L^{2}_{p}}+{\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}/2}{\bf\{I-P\}}(f-h)\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{T_{0}}L^{2}_{p}}}\\
\lesssim\|{f}_{1}-{h}_{1}\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{p}}+{\|{t^{3\mathfrak{q}}(f-h)}\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{\infty}_{T_{0}}L^{2}_{p}}}\left({\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}/2}f\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{T_{0}}L^{2}_{p}}}+{\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}/2}h\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{T_{0}}L^{2}_{p}}}\right).

Since
f
and
h
satisfy (
6.2
) with
\epsilon_{0}>0
sufficiently small we obtain

\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}}(f-h)\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}+{\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}/2}{\bf\{I-P\}}(f-h)\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}\lesssim\|{f}_{1}-{h}_{1}\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{p}}.

The uniqueness of the local-in-time solution follows directly.

We now explain how to apply the proof of positivity, as in
[
85
]
, to the massless Boltzmann equation on FLRW in (
1.8
). For this, we use the alternative approximating formula

(6.8)

\partial_{t}F^{n+1}+\frac{p^{i}}{|t^{\mathfrak{q}}p|}\partial_{x^{i}}F^{n+1}-\frac{2\mathfrak{q}}{t}p^{i}\partial_{p^{i}}F^{n+1}+R(F^{n})F^{n+1}=Q_{+}(F^{n},F^{n}),

for
n\in\{0,1,2,\ldots\}

with the initial conditions

\left.F^{n+1}\right|_{t=1}=F_{1}(x,p)=J(p)+\sqrt{J(p)}f_{1}(x,p)\geq 0.

We also take

(6.9)

F^{0}(t,x,p)\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)\geq 0,\quad t\geq 1.

In (
6.8
) we have used the standard decomposition of

Q=Q_{+}-Q_{-}

into gain and loss terms where from (
1.30
) we have

\begin{split}Q_{+}(F_{1},F_{2})=&\penalty\ t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}{dq}\penalty\ \int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}d\omega\penalty\ v_{\text{\o}}\penalty\ F_{1}(p^{\prime})F_{2}(q^{\prime}),\\
Q_{-}(F_{1},F_{2})=&\penalty\ t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}{dq}\penalty\ \int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}d\omega\penalty\ v_{\text{\o}}\penalty\ F_{1}(p)F_{2}(q)=R(F_{2})F_{1}(p),\end{split}

where

\displaystyle R(F_{2})\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}Q_{-}(1,F_{2})=t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}{dq}\penalty\ \int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}d\omega\penalty\ v_{\text{\o}}\penalty\ F_{2}(q).

We will also use the notation
Q_{+}^{n}\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}Q_{+}(F^{n},F^{n})
.

The characteristics of (
1.8
) and (
1.35
) for
s\geq 1
are given by

\begin{split}\frac{d{X}^{j}}{ds}=&\penalty\ s^{-\mathfrak{q}}\frac{{P}^{j}}{|{P}|},\qquad{X}^{j}(s=1,x,p)=x^{j},\\
\frac{d{P}^{j}}{ds}=&\penalty\ -\frac{2\mathfrak{q}}{s}P^{j},\qquad{P}^{j}(s=1,p)=p^{j}.\end{split}

For
0\leq\mathfrak{q}<1
we define

(6.10)

\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{q}}(t)\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}\frac{t^{1-\mathfrak{q}}-1}{1-\mathfrak{q}},\qquad\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{q}}(1)=0.

For
\mathfrak{q}=1
we define

(6.11)

\mathcal{S}_{1}(t)\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}\log(t),\qquad\mathcal{S}_{1}(1)=0.

These characteristics are solved as

(6.12)

\begin{split}{X}^{j}(t,x,p)=&\penalty\ x^{j}+\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{q}}(t)\frac{p^{j}}{|p|},\\
{P}^{j}(t,p)=&\penalty\ {t^{-2\mathfrak{q}}}p^{j}.\end{split}

We will use (
6.12
) to express solutions to (
6.8
) as an ordinary differential equation.

We first consider the linear model of (
1.35
) with a source term
{\mathcal{H}}
as

(6.13)

\partial_{t}F+\frac{p^{i}}{|t^{\mathfrak{q}}p|}\partial_{x^{i}}F-\frac{2\mathfrak{q}}{t}p^{i}\partial_{p^{i}}F={\mathcal{H}}(t,x,p),\quad F(t=1,x,p)=F_{1}(x,p).

Then with (
6.12
) solutions to (
6.13
) satisfy

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left(F(t,{X}(t,x,p),{P}(t,p))\right)={\mathcal{H}}(t,{X}(t,x,p),{P}(t,p)).

Integrating over
[1,t]
we obtain

\displaystyle F(t,{X}(t),{P}(t))=

\displaystyle\penalty\ F_{1}({X}(1),{P}(1))+\int_{1}^{t}d\tau\penalty\ {\mathcal{H}}(\tau,{X}(\tau),{P}(\tau)).

Equivalently,

\displaystyle F\left(t,x+\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{q}}(t)\frac{p}{|p|},{t^{-2\mathfrak{q}}}p\right)=

\displaystyle\penalty\ F_{1}(x,p)

\displaystyle\penalty\ +\int_{1}^{t}d\tau\penalty\ {\mathcal{H}}\left(\tau,x+\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{q}}(\tau)\frac{p}{|p|},{\tau^{-2\mathfrak{q}}}p\right).

Next, setting
q={t^{-2\mathfrak{q}}}p
and
y=x+\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{q}}(t)\frac{p}{|p|}=x+\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{q}}(t)\frac{q}{|q|}
, we equivalently have

\displaystyle F\left(t,y,q\right)=

\displaystyle\penalty\ F_{1}\left(t,y-\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{q}}(t)\frac{q}{|q|},t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q\right)

\displaystyle\penalty\ +\int_{1}^{t}d\tau\penalty\ {\mathcal{H}}\left(\tau,y+\left(\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{q}}(\tau)-\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{q}}(t)\right)\frac{q}{|q|},{(t/\tau)^{2\mathfrak{q}}}q\right).

Thus, for
t\geq\tau\geq 1
, we define the backward characteristics of (
6.12
) by

(6.14)

\begin{split}\widetilde{X}^{j}(t,\tau,x,p)=&\penalty\ x^{j}+\left(\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{q}}(\tau)-\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{q}}(t)\right)\frac{p^{j}}{|p|},\\
\widetilde{P}^{j}(t,\tau,p)=&\penalty\ {(t/\tau)^{2\mathfrak{q}}}p^{j}.\end{split}

Now, by using (
6.14
), we define the operator
U(t,\tau)
for
t\geq\tau\geq 1
by

(U(t,\tau)G)(s,x,p)\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}G(s,\widetilde{X}(t,\tau,x,p),\widetilde{P}(t,\tau,p)),\quad s\geq 1.

Then we can write the solution to (
6.13
) as

\displaystyle F(t,x,p)=(U(t,1)F_{1})(x,p)+\int_{1}^{t}d\tau\penalty\ (U(t,\tau){\mathcal{H}})(\tau,x,p).

Next we will apply this calculation to (
6.8
).

We use the source
{\mathcal{H}}=Q_{+}(F^{n},F^{n})=Q_{+}^{n}
in (
6.13
) to write (
6.8
) as

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left(F^{n+1}(t,{X}(t),{P}(t))\right)+R(F^{n})F^{n+1}(t,{X}(t),{P}(t))={Q_{+}^{n}}(t,{X}(t),{P}(t)).

Further with (
6.12
) we define

\displaystyle\Phi^{n}(t,s,x,p)\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}

\displaystyle\penalty\ \int_{s}^{t}d\tau\penalty\ R(F^{n})(\tau,{X}(\tau),{P}(\tau)),\quad\Phi^{n}(t)=\Phi^{n}(t,1,x,p).

Then we can write (
6.8
) as

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left(e^{\Phi^{n}(t)}F^{n+1}(t,{X}(t),{P}(t))\right)=e^{\Phi^{n}(t)}{Q_{+}^{n}}(t,{X}(t),{P}(t)).

We integrate over
[1,t]
to obtain

\displaystyle F^{n+1}\left(t,x+\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{q}}(t)\frac{p}{|p|},{t^{-2\mathfrak{q}}}p\right)=

\displaystyle\penalty\ e^{-\Phi^{n}(t,1,x,p)}F_{1}(x,p)

\displaystyle\penalty\ +\int_{1}^{t}d\tau\penalty\ e^{-\Phi^{n}(t,\tau,x,p)}{Q_{+}^{n}}\left(\tau,x+\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{q}}(\tau)\frac{p}{|p|},{\tau^{-2\mathfrak{q}}}p\right).

Above for
t\geq\tau\geq 1
, we have used that

\Phi^{n}(\tau)-\Phi^{n}(t)=\Phi^{n}(\tau,1,x,p)-\Phi^{n}(t,1,x,p)=-\Phi^{n}(t,\tau,x,p).

Further with (
6.14
) we define

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Phi}^{n}(t,s,x,p)\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}

\displaystyle\penalty\ \int_{s}^{t}d\tau\penalty\ R(F^{n})(\tau,\widetilde{X}(t,\tau,x,p),\widetilde{P}(t,\tau,p)),\quad\widetilde{\Phi}^{n}(t)=\widetilde{\Phi}^{n}(t,1,x,p).

Then using the backward characteristics as in (
6.14
) we can write (
6.8
) as

(6.15)

F^{n+1}(t,x,p)=e^{-\widetilde{\Phi}^{n}(t)}(U(t,1)F_{1})(x,p)\\
+\int_{1}^{t}d\tau\penalty\ e^{-\widetilde{\Phi}^{n}(t,\tau)}(U(t,\tau){Q_{+}^{n}})(\tau,x,p).

Now we use (
6.15
) to give the proof of the positivity of a solution to (
6.8
).

Indeed given
F^{n}\geq 0
, then clearly
Q_{+}^{n}=Q_{+}(F^{n},F^{n})\geq 0
. Then (
6.15
) and
F_{1}(x,p)\geq 0
immediately implies that
F^{n+1}(t,x,p)\geq 0
. From (
6.9
) we have that
F^{0}(t,x,p)\geq 0
. Thus, by induction
F^{n+1}(t,x,p)\geq 0
, for all
n\geq 0
if
F_{1}\geq 0
.

Next we will show that in the limit as
n\to\infty
that

(6.16)

F^{n+1}(t,x,p)\to F(t,x,p)=J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)+\sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)}f(t,x,p)\geq 0,

and that this is the same local-in-time solution we previously obtained. We prove convergence in the space
L^{1}_{{k}}L^{\infty}_{T_{0}}L^{2}_{p}
. However, due to the embedding

L^{1}_{{k}}L^{\infty}_{T_{0}}L^{2}_{p}\subset L^{\infty}([1,T_{0}]\times\mathbb{T}^{3}_{x};L^{2}_{p})

we also have convergence in
L^{\infty}((0,T_{0})\times\mathbb{T}^{3};L^{2}_{p})
. Thus we have the almost everywhere non-negativity in the limit (
6.16
). To prove the convergence, for

F^{n+1}(t,x,p)=J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)+\sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)}f^{n+1}(t,x,p),

we can rewrite (
6.8
) as

(6.17)

\left(\partial_{t}+\frac{p^{i}}{|t^{\mathfrak{q}}p|}\partial_{x^{i}}-\frac{2\mathfrak{q}}{t}p^{i}\partial_{p^{i}}+t^{-3\mathfrak{q}}\nu_{0}\right)f^{n+1}\\
=\Gamma_{+}(f^{n},f^{n})-\Gamma_{-}(f^{n+1},f^{n})+\mathcal{K}(f^{n}).

Here,
\mathcal{K}
is defined in (
1.39
) and then instead of (
1.22
) we use

\displaystyle\Gamma_{+}(h_{1},h_{2})

\displaystyle\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\penalty\ dq\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\penalty\ d\omega\penalty\ v_{\text{\o}}\penalty\ \sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)}h_{1}(p^{\prime})h_{2}(q^{\prime}),

\displaystyle\Gamma_{-}(h_{1},h_{2})

\displaystyle\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\penalty\ dq\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\penalty\ d\omega\penalty\ v_{\text{\o}}\penalty\ \sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}q)}h_{1}(p)h_{2}(q).

Similar to (
6.5
),
the iteration scheme (
6.17
) satisfies the uniform estimate in (
6.6
) and it is a Cauchy sequence with the same proofs. Thus the limit function of (
6.17
) is a weak solution of (
1.19
) satisfying (
6.2
). Since we have uniqueness, then the two solutions coincide. The proof of Theorem
6.1
is complete.
∎

6.2.
Global existence near the equilibrium

We will now prove the global-in-time uniform decay estimate (
1.42
) from Theorem
1.4
with
l=0
.

Theorem 6.3
.

Let
\mathfrak{q}\in[0,1]
. Under the assumptions of Theorem
1.4
, there is a unique global solution
f
of (
1.19
). Moreover, the following estimate holds,

(6.18)

\displaystyle\forall T\geq 1,\qquad{\|{t^{3\mathfrak{q}}f}\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}+{\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}/2}f\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}\lesssim\big\|{f}_{1}\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{p}}.

Proof.

To this end, we first estimate the last term on the right side of (
4.1
). From (
5.11
), we have

\displaystyle\left\langle{\hat{\Gamma}(\hat{f},\hat{f})(k)},{t^{3\mathfrak{q}}{\mathfrak{e}_{\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)}}\right\rangle=\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\left\langle{\Gamma(\hat{f}(k-l),\hat{f}(l))},{t^{3\mathfrak{q}}{\mathfrak{e}_{\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)}}\right\rangle d\Sigma(l).

Then from Lemma
3.8
we have the bound

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle{\hat{\Gamma}(\hat{f},\hat{f})(t,k)},{t^{3\mathfrak{q}}{\mathfrak{e}_{\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)}}\right\rangle\right|^{2}\lesssim\left(\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\|\hat{f}(k-l)\|_{L^{2}_{p}}\|\hat{f}(l)\|_{L^{2}_{p}}d\Sigma(l)\right)^{2}.

Then exactly as in (
5.9
) and the estimates below it, we obtain that

\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}_{k}}\left(\int^{T}_{1}t^{5\mathfrak{q}}\left|\left\langle{\hat{\Gamma}(\hat{f},\hat{f})(t,k)},{t^{3\mathfrak{q}}{\mathfrak{e}_{\ell}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)}}\right\rangle\right|^{2}dt\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}d\Sigma(k)\\
\lesssim\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}}{f}\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}/2}{f}\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}.

In fact, a lower time weight is possible in the above estimate.
We place this estimate into (
4.1
) to obtain

(6.19)

\displaystyle\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}/2}[\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B},\mathcal{C}]\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{T}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}}f\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}+\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}/2}{\bf\{I-P\}}f\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}+\|f_{1}\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{p}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}}{f}\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}/2}{f}\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}.

We multiply a small constant to the estimate of
\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}/2}[\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B},\mathcal{C}]\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{T}}
in (
6.19
) and then add that to (
5.1
) to achieve that

\displaystyle{\|{t^{3\mathfrak{q}}f}\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}+{\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}/2}f\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}\lesssim\big\|{f}_{1}\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{p}}+{\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}}f\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}{\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}/2}f\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}.

Thus, if we start with a sufficiently small
\big\|{f}_{1}\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{p}}
, then
{\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}}f\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}
will remain small for a short period of time, and we obtain (
6.18
). Then, the above estimate (
6.18
) can be propagated for long times. This provides our global uniform decay estimate for any
0\leq\mathfrak{q}\leq 1
. Then, we obtain the global-in-time existence and uniqueness of the solution using a standard continuity argument.
∎

6.3.
Faster time-decay rates for
0<\mathfrak{q}\leq\frac{1}{3}

Let us now prove the faster decay estimate (
1.43
) in Theorem
1.4
with the weights (
1.44
)
1
and (
1.44
)
2
when
m=0
.

Proposition 6.4
.

Let
0<\mathfrak{q}\leq\frac{1}{3}
and fix
0<\vartheta<1
. Under the assumptions of Theorem
1.4
, there holds

(6.20)

\displaystyle\forall T\geq 1,\qquad{\|{t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}(t)f}\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}+{\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}/2}\mathcal{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}(t)f\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}\lesssim\big\|{f}_{1}\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{p}}.

Proof.

First, we use (
1.21
) and Proposition
1.3
to write (
5.3
) as

\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\left(t^{6\mathfrak{q}}\|\hat{f}\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}(t,k)\right)+\vartheta\frac{\nu_{0}}{t^{3\mathfrak{q}}}\left(t^{6\mathfrak{q}}\|\hat{f}\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}(t,k)\right)+(1-\vartheta)\frac{\nu_{0}}{t^{3\mathfrak{q}}}\left(t^{6\mathfrak{q}}\|\hat{f}\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}(t,k)\right)\\
=t^{6\mathfrak{q}}\real{\left\langle{\hat{\Gamma}(\hat{f},\hat{f})},{\hat{f}}\right\rangle}-t^{6\mathfrak{q}}\real{\left\langle{\mathcal{K}\hat{f}},{\hat{f}}\right\rangle}.

Now we recall the time weight (
1.44
) for the case of
0<\mathfrak{q}\leq\frac{1}{3}
as

\mathcal{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}(t)=\begin{cases}\exp\left(\vartheta\frac{\nu_{0}t^{1-3\mathfrak{q}}}{1-3\mathfrak{q}}\right),\quad&\text{if}\,\,0<\mathfrak{q}<\frac{1}{3},\\
t^{\vartheta\nu_{0}},\qquad&\text{if}\,\,\mathfrak{q}=\frac{1}{3}.\end{cases}

Then similar to (
1.51
) we have

\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\left(t^{6\mathfrak{q}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}(t)^{2}\|\hat{f}\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}(t,k)\right)+(1-\vartheta)\frac{\nu_{0}}{t^{3\mathfrak{q}}}\left(t^{6\mathfrak{q}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}(t)^{2}\|\hat{f}\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}(t,k)\right)\\
=t^{6\mathfrak{q}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}(t)^{2}\left\{\real{\left\langle{\hat{\Gamma}(\hat{f},\hat{f})},{\hat{f}}\right\rangle}-\real{\left\langle{\mathcal{K}\hat{f}},{\hat{f}}\right\rangle}\right\}.

Next integrating from
[1,t]
as in (
5.4
) we have

\frac{1}{2}t^{6\mathfrak{q}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}(t)^{2}\|\hat{f}\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}(t,k)+(1-\vartheta)\nu_{0}\int^{t}_{1}\tau^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}(\tau)^{2}\|\hat{f}(\tau,k)\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}d\tau\\
=\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}(1)\|\hat{f}_{1}(k)\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}+\int^{t}_{1}\tau^{6\mathfrak{q}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}(\tau)^{2}\left(\real{\left\langle{\hat{\Gamma}(\hat{f},\hat{f})},{\hat{f}}\right\rangle}-\real{\left\langle{\mathcal{K}\hat{f}},{\hat{f}}\right\rangle}\right)d\tau.

Then following the arguments in (
4.24
) and (
5.5
) then instead of (
5.6
) we obtain for some uniform constant
C_{1}>0
that

{\|{t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}(t)f}\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}+{\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}/2}\mathcal{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}(t)f\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}\leq C_{1}\big\|{f}_{1}\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{p}}\\
+C_{1}\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\left(\int^{T}_{1}\tau^{6\mathfrak{q}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}(\tau)^{2}\left|\left\langle{\mathcal{K}\hat{f}},{\hat{f}}\right\rangle\right|d\tau\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}d\Sigma(k)\\
+C_{1}\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\left(\int^{T}_{1}\tau^{6\mathfrak{q}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}(\tau)^{2}\left|{\left\langle{\hat{\Gamma}(\hat{f},\hat{f})},{\hat{f}}\right\rangle}\right|d\tau\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}d\Sigma(k).

We next estimate the terms involving
\left\langle{\mathcal{K}\hat{f}},{\hat{f}}\right\rangle
and
{\left\langle{\hat{\Gamma}(\hat{f},\hat{f})},{\hat{f}}\right\rangle}
.

We first estimate
{\left\langle{\hat{\Gamma}(\hat{f},\hat{f})},{\hat{f}}\right\rangle}
exactly as in (
5.7
). Then similarly to (
5.8
) we apply Cauchy–Schwarz’s inequality to
\int_{1}^{T}(\cdot)dt
and use Young’s inequality with small constant
\eta>0
to obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}_{k}}\left(\int^{T}_{1}t^{6\mathfrak{q}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}(t)^{2}\big\|\hat{f}(t,k)\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}}\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}_{l}}\|\hat{f}(t,k-l)\|_{L^{2}_{p}}\big\|\hat{f}(t,l)\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}}d\Sigma(l)dt\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}d\Sigma(k)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}_{k}}\left(\int^{T}_{1}t^{6\mathfrak{q}}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}(t)^{2}\left(\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}_{l}}\|\hat{f}(t,k-l)\|_{L^{2}_{p}}\big\|\hat{f}(t,l)\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}}d\Sigma(l)\right)^{2}dt\right)^{1/4}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\left(\int^{T}_{1}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}(t)^{2}\big\|\hat{f}(t,k)\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}dt\right)^{1/4}d\Sigma(k)

(6.21)

\displaystyle\leq\eta\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}_{k}}\left(\int^{T}_{1}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}(t)^{2}\big\|\hat{f}(t,k)\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}dt\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}d\Sigma(k)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{4\eta}\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}_{k}}\left(\int^{T}_{1}t^{6\mathfrak{q}}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}(t)^{2}\left(\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}_{l}}\|\hat{f}(t,k-l)\|_{L^{2}_{p}}\big\|\hat{f}(t,l)\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}}d\Sigma(l)\right)^{2}dt\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}d\Sigma(k).

For the second term on the right-hand side of (
6.21
),
we use (
5.9
) to obtain

\left(\int^{T}_{1}t^{6\mathfrak{q}}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}(t)^{2}\left(\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}_{l}}\|\hat{f}(t,k-l)\|_{L^{2}_{p}}\big\|\hat{f}(t,l)\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}}d\Sigma(l)\right)^{2}dt\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
\leq\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}_{l}}\left(\int^{T}_{1}t^{6\mathfrak{q}}\|\hat{f}(t,k-l)\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}(t)^{2}\big\|\hat{f}(t,l)\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}dt\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}d\Sigma(l)\\
\leq\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}_{k}}\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}_{l}}\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq T}\left(\tau^{3\mathfrak{q}}\|\hat{f}(\tau,k-l)\|_{L^{2}_{p}}\right)\left(\int^{T}_{1}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}(t)^{2}\big\|\hat{f}(t,l)\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}dt\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}d\Sigma(l)d\Sigma(k).

Then Fubini’s theorem and translation invariance imply that

\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}_{k}}\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}_{l}}\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq T}\left(\tau^{3\mathfrak{q}}\|\hat{f}(\tau,k-l)\|_{L^{2}_{p}}\right)\left(\int^{T}_{1}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}(t)^{2}\big\|\hat{f}(t,l)\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}dt\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}d\Sigma(l)d\Sigma(k)\\
=\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}}{f}\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}/2}\mathcal{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}(t){f}\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}.

Then the estimate above with (
6.21
) proves the following uniform estimate

(6.22)

\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\left(\int^{T}_{1}\tau^{6\mathfrak{q}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}(\tau)^{2}\left|{\left\langle{\hat{\Gamma}(\hat{f},\hat{f})},{\hat{f}}\right\rangle}\right|d\tau\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}d\Sigma(k)\\
\lesssim\eta\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}/2}\mathcal{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}(t){f}\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}+\frac{1}{4\eta}\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}}{f}\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}/2}\mathcal{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}(t){f}\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}.

This will be our main estimate for
{\left\langle{\hat{\Gamma}(\hat{f},\hat{f})},{\hat{f}}\right\rangle}
.

Then first choosing
\eta>0
sufficiently small and second choosing
\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}}{f_{1}}\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}
sufficiently small so that
\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}}{f}\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}
remains sufficiently small, we obtain for some different uniform constant
C_{1}>0
that

(6.23)

{\|{t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}(t)f}\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}+{\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}/2}\mathcal{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}(t)f\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}\leq C_{1}\big\|{f}_{1}\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{p}}\\
+C_{1}\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\left(\int^{T}_{1}\tau^{6\mathfrak{q}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}(\tau)^{2}\left|\left\langle{\mathcal{K}\hat{f}},{\hat{f}}\right\rangle\right|d\tau\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}d\Sigma(k).

We next estimate the term including
\left\langle{\mathcal{K}\hat{f}},{\hat{f}}\right\rangle
. As in Proposition
3.1
we split
\mathcal{K}=\mathcal{K}_{c}+\mathcal{K}_{s}
. For the
\mathcal{K}_{s}
part, from Proposition
3.1
, for any small
\eta>0
we have the estimate

\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\left(\int^{T}_{1}\tau^{6\mathfrak{q}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}(\tau)^{2}\left|\left\langle{\mathcal{K}_{s}\hat{f}},{\hat{f}}\right\rangle\right|d\tau\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}d\Sigma(k)\\
\leq\eta\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\left(\int^{T}_{1}\tau^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}(\tau)^{2}\|\hat{f}(\tau,k)\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}d\tau\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}d\Sigma(k)=\eta{\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}/2}\mathcal{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}(t)f\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}.

Then, for the
\mathcal{K}_{c}
part, we use (
3.1
) with
\zeta=\frac{1}{3}
to obtain

\displaystyle t^{3\mathfrak{q}}|\langle\mathcal{K}_{c}h_{1},h_{2}\rangle|\lesssim t^{-\mathfrak{q}}\|{\bf 1}_{\leq R}h_{1}\|_{L^{2}_{p}}\|{\bf 1}_{\leq R}h_{2}\|_{L^{2}_{p}}.

Then we have

\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\left(\int^{T}_{1}\tau^{6\mathfrak{q}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}(\tau)^{2}\left|\left\langle{\mathcal{K}_{c}\hat{f}},{\hat{f}}\right\rangle\right|d\tau\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}d\Sigma(k)\\
\leq C_{\eta}\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\left(\int^{T}_{1}\tau^{3\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{q}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}(\tau)^{2}\|\hat{f}(\tau,k)\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}d\tau\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}d\Sigma(k).

Next we suppose that
T>0
is a large time and we notice that the constant
C_{\eta}>0
above is independent of
T
. Then fix a small
\delta>0
and choose a time
T_{1}<T
such that we have

\displaystyle C_{\eta}\tau^{-\mathfrak{q}}<\delta,\quad\forall\tau\geq T_{1}.

On the other hand region we bound

\displaystyle\tau^{-\mathfrak{q}/2}\mathcal{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}(\tau)\leq\mathcal{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}(T_{1})\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}C_{\delta},\quad\forall 1\leq\tau<T_{1}.

Collecting these estimates we obtain for any small
\delta>0
that

\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\left(\int^{T}_{1}\tau^{6\mathfrak{q}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}(\tau)^{2}\left|\left\langle{\mathcal{K}_{c}\hat{f}},{\hat{f}}\right\rangle\right|d\tau\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}d\Sigma(k)\\
\leq\delta{\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}/2}\mathcal{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}(t)f\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}+C_{\delta}{\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}/2}f\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}.

The large constant
C_{\delta}>0
is uniform for
T>T_{1}
. We collect these estimates to obtain

\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\left(\int^{T}_{1}\tau^{6\mathfrak{q}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}(\tau)^{2}\left|\left\langle{\mathcal{K}\hat{f}},{\hat{f}}\right\rangle\right|d\tau\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}d\Sigma(k)\\
\leq\eta{\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}/2}\mathcal{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}(t)f\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}+\delta{\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}/2}\mathcal{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}(t)f\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}+C_{\delta}{\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}/2}f\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}.

We plug this estimate into (
6.23
) choosing
\eta,\delta>0
sufficiently small to obtain

\displaystyle{\|{t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}(t)f}\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}+{\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}/2}\mathcal{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}(t)f\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}\leq C_{2}\big\|{f}_{1}\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{p}}+C_{2}{\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}/2}f\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}.

Here
C_{2}>0
is some large uniform constant. Lastly we apply the inequality (
6.18
) to the above to obtain the uniform decay estimate (
6.20
).
This proves the faster decay rates represented by the weight (
1.44
)
1
when
m=0
. The case of the weight (
1.44
)
2
when
m=0
is proven by the same method.
∎

6.4.
Exponential large time decay for
\mathfrak{q}=0

We now prove the large time decay estimates (
1.43
) in the non-expanding case when
\mathfrak{q}=0
.

Proposition 6.5
.

Let
\mathfrak{q}=0
. Under the assumptions of Theorem
1.4
, there is a small uniform constant
\lambda>0
such that

(6.24)

\displaystyle\forall T\geq 1,\qquad\big\|e^{\lambda t}f\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}+{\big\|e^{\lambda t}f\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}\lesssim\|{f}_{1}\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{p}}.

Proof.

For a small constant
\lambda>0
we consider
\hat{h}(t,k,p)=e^{\lambda t}\hat{f}(t,k,p)
with
{h}_{1}=e^{\lambda}{f}_{1}
. Then we can re-write (
5.2
) as

\displaystyle\left(\partial_{t}+i\frac{p}{|t^{\mathfrak{q}}p|}\cdot k-\frac{2\mathfrak{q}}{t}p^{i}\partial_{p^{i}}\right)\hat{h}+\mathcal{L}\hat{h}=e^{-\lambda t}\hat{\Gamma}(\hat{h},\hat{h})+\lambda\hat{h}.

Then following the proof of (
5.1
) we obtain

\displaystyle\big\|h\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}+{\big\|{\bf\{I-P\}}h\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}\lesssim\|{f}_{1}\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{p}}+{\|{h}\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}{\big\|h\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}+\lambda{\big\|h\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}.

Furthermore, directly following the proof of (
4.1
) and (
6.19
), for a sufficiently small constant
\lambda>0
when
\mathfrak{q}=0
we have

\displaystyle\big\|e^{\lambda t}[\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B},\mathcal{C}]\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{T}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|h\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}+\|{\bf\{I-P\}}h\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}+\|h_{1}\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{p}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\big\|h\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}\big\|h\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}.

Collecting these estimates for
\|{f}_{1}\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{p}}
and
\lambda>0
sufficiently small we obtain the decay estimate (
6.24
). This proves the exponential time decay when
\mathfrak{q}=0
.
∎

6.5.
Time-decay of weighted norms

Let us prove the decay estimates for weighted norms (
1.43
) using the weight
w_{m}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)
defined in (
1.41
).

Proposition 6.6
.

Let
\mathfrak{q}\in[0,1]
and
m\geq 0
. Under the assumptions of Theorem
1.4
, there holds

(6.25)

\displaystyle\forall t\geq 1,\qquad\||t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p|^{m/2}{f}(t)\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{p}}\lesssim t^{-3\mathfrak{q}}\|w_{m}{f}\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{p}}(1).

Proof.

We multiply (
5.2
) by
w_{m}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)^{2}
in (
1.41
) and use the invariance in (
1.9
) to obtain

(6.26)

\left(\partial_{t}+i\frac{p}{|t^{\mathfrak{q}}p|}\cdot k-\frac{2\mathfrak{q}}{t}p^{i}\partial_{p^{i}}\right)\left(w_{m}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)^{2}\hat{f}(t,k,p)\right)\\
+w_{m}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)^{2}\mathcal{L}\hat{f}(t,k,p)=w_{m}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)^{2}\hat{\Gamma}(\hat{f},\hat{f})(t,k,p).

Then following the proof of Proposition
5.1
we obtain instead of (
5.1
) that

(6.27)

\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}}w_{m}f\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}+\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\left(\int^{T}_{1}\tau^{6\mathfrak{q}}w_{m}(\tau^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)^{2}\real{\left\langle{\mathcal{L}\hat{f}},{\hat{f}}\right\rangle}(t,k)d\tau\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}d\Sigma(k)\\
\lesssim\|w_{m}{f}\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{p}}(1)+{\|{t^{3\mathfrak{q}}w_{m}f}\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}{\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}/2}w_{m}f\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}.

We apply now Corollary
3.2
to obtain

(6.28)

\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}}w_{m}f\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}+{\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}/2}w_{m}f\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}\\
\lesssim\|w_{m}{f}\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{p}}(1)+{\|{t^{3\mathfrak{q}}w_{m}f}\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}{\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}/2}w_{m}f\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}+\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}/2}f\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}.

Next we use (
6.18
) to obtain
\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}/2}f\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}\lesssim\big\|{f}_{1}\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{p}}\lesssim\|w_{m}{f}\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{p}}(1)
. Then if we further start with a sufficiently small
\|w_{m}{f}\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{p}}(1)
norm, then the norm
{\|{t^{3\mathfrak{q}}w_{m}f}\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}
will remain small for a short period of time, so that we obtain

(6.29)

\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}}w_{m}f\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}+{\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}/2}w_{m}f\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}\lesssim\|w_{m}{f}\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{p}}(1).

Then we obtain from (
6.29
) the rapid decay rates stated in (
6.25
).
∎

Remark 6.7
.

The weights
w_{m}(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)
can be directly added to the proofs of the faster time decay rates in the previous subsections to obtain the estimates in (
1.43
).

6.6.
Propagation of spatial regularity

We next prove the propagation of spatial regularity for solutions to (
1.19
)–(
1.20
). We study the Boltzmann equation in Fourier space as in (
5.2
). And for
l>0
, we multiply (
5.2
) by
\langle k\rangle^{l}
, and prove analogous global uniform bounds to propagate this frequency weight and thereby show the propagation of spatial regularity.

Proposition 6.8
.

Let
\mathfrak{q}\in[0,1]
. Under the assumptions of Theorem
1.4
, there holds

(6.30)

\displaystyle\forall T\geq 1,\qquad{\|{t^{3\mathfrak{q}}f}\|_{L^{1}_{k,l}L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}+\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}/2}f\|_{L^{1}_{k,l}L^{2}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}\lesssim\|{f}_{1}\|_{L^{1}_{k,l}L^{2}_{p}}.

The following proof follows closely the argument in
[
20
]
.

Proof.

Following the proofs of Lemma
5.2
and Theorem
4.1
, one can prove that

\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\bigg(\int_{1}^{T}\big|\left\langle{\hat{\Gamma}(\hat{f},\hat{g})},{\langle k\rangle^{2l}\,\hat{h}}\right\rangle\big|dt\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}d\Sigma(k)\leq C_{\eta}\|f\|_{L^{1}_{k,l}L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}\|g\|_{L^{1}_{k,l}L^{2}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}\\
+C_{\eta}\|f\|_{L^{1}_{k,l}L^{2}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}\|g\|_{L^{1}_{k,l}L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}+\eta\|h\|_{L^{1}_{k,l}L^{2}_{T}L^{2}_{p}},

and also using the argument from (
6.19
) we have

\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}/2}[\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B},\mathcal{C}]\big\|_{L^{1}_{k,l}L^{2}_{T}}\lesssim\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}/2}{\bf\{I-P\}}f\|_{L^{1}_{k,l}L^{2}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}+\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}}f\|_{L^{1}_{k,l}L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}\\
+\|f_{1}\|_{L^{1}_{k,l}L^{2}_{p}}+\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}}{f}\big\|_{L^{1}_{k,l}L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}/2}{f}\big\|_{L^{1}_{k,l}L^{2}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}.

Next, taking the
L^{2}_{p}
inner product of (
5.2
) with the complex conjugate of
\langle k\rangle^{2l}\hat{f}
and then taking the real part, we obtain, similarly to (
5.3
), that

(6.31)

\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\left(t^{6\mathfrak{q}}\langle k\rangle^{2l}\|\hat{f}\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}(t,k)\right)+t^{6\mathfrak{q}}\real{\left\langle{\mathcal{L}\hat{f}},{\langle k\rangle^{2l}\hat{f}}\right\rangle}\\
=t^{6\mathfrak{q}}\real{\left\langle{\hat{\Gamma}(\hat{f},\hat{f})},{\langle k\rangle^{2l}\hat{f}}\right\rangle}.

Then following exactly the proof of Proposition
5.1
, for any small
\eta>0
, we have

{\|{t^{3\mathfrak{q}}f}\|_{L^{1}_{k,l}L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}+\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}/2}{\bf\{I-P\}}f\|_{L^{1}_{k,l}L^{2}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}\\
\lesssim\|{f}_{1}\|_{L^{1}_{k,l}L^{2}_{p}}+\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\left(\int^{T}_{1}t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\big|\left\langle{\hat{\Gamma}(\hat{f},\hat{f})},{\langle k\rangle^{2l}{\bf\{I-P\}}\hat{f}}\right\rangle\big|\,dt\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}d\Sigma(k)\\
\lesssim\|{f}_{1}\|_{L^{1}_{k,l}L^{2}_{p}}+C_{\eta}{\|{t^{3\mathfrak{q}}f}\|_{L^{1}_{k,l}L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}/2}{f}\|_{L^{1}_{k,l}L^{2}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}+\eta\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}/2}{\bf\{I-P\}}f\|_{L^{1}_{k,l}L^{2}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}.

For
\|{f}_{1}\|_{L^{1}_{k,l}L^{2}_{p}}
sufficiently small, after a short time
{\|{t^{3\mathfrak{q}}f}\|_{L^{1}_{k,l}L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}
remains small. Collecting the estimates above, we obtain for
\eta>0
sufficiently small that the estimate (
6.30
) holds. This establishes the propagation of spatial regularity for any
l>0
and for
\|{f}_{1}\|_{L^{1}_{k,l}L^{2}_{p}}
sufficiently small.
∎

7.
Global stability of the vacuum solution

In this section, we will prove Theorem
1.9
. In Section
7.1
, we first show the local well-posedness result Theorem
1.9
is based on. In Section
7.2
, we show global existence and the uniform decay estimate (
1.47
) in Theorem
1.9
.

7.1.
Local existence and uniqueness around the vacuum

We state here local existence and uniqueness of solutions for the massless Boltzmann equation on FLRW spacetime (
1.19
) around vacuum.

Theorem 7.1

(Local existence and uniqueness near vacuum)
.

Let
\mathfrak{q}\in[0,1]
. Under the assumptions of Theorem
1.9
, there is a small
\epsilon_{0}>0
, a
T_{0}=T_{0}(\epsilon_{0})>0
and some
C_{0}=C_{0}(T_{0})>0
such that if

\|f_{1}\|_{L_{k}^{1}L^{2}_{p}}\leq\epsilon_{0},

then the massless Boltzmann equation on FLRW spacetime around vacuum, (
1.45
),
admits a unique local-in-time solution

f(t,x,p),\penalty\ \penalty\ \ 0\leq t\leq T_{0}<\infty,\penalty\ \penalty\ \ x\in\mathbb{T}^{3},\penalty\ \penalty\ \ p\in\mathbb{R}^{3},

which satisfies the uniform estimate

\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}}f\|_{L^{1}_{{k}}L^{\infty}_{T_{0}}L^{2}_{p}}\leq C_{0}\|f_{1}\|_{L_{{k}}^{1}L^{2}_{p}}.

If additionally

F_{1}(x,p)=J^{\frac{1}{2}}(p)f_{1}(x,p)\geq 0
,
then the solution satisfies

F(t,{x},p)=\sqrt{J(t^{2\mathfrak{q}}p)}f(t,{x},p)\geq 0.

The previous theorem is obtained by the same arguments performed in the proof of Theorem
6.1
concerning local existence and uniqueness of solutions for the massless Boltzmann around the Maxwell–Jüttner equilibrium.

7.2.
Global existence near the vacuum solution

We will now prove the global-in-time uniform decay estimate (
1.47
) from Theorem
1.9
.

Theorem 7.2
.

Let
\mathfrak{q}\in(\frac{1}{3},1]
. Under the assumptions of Theorem
1.9
, there is a unique global solution
f
of (
1.45
). Moreover, the following estimate holds,

\forall T\geq 1,\qquad{\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}}f\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}\lesssim\big\|f_{1}\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{p}}.

Proof.

Now take the Fourier transform of (
1.45
) to obtain

(7.1)

\displaystyle\left(\partial_{t}+i\frac{p}{|t^{\mathfrak{q}}p|}\cdot k-\frac{2\mathfrak{q}}{t}p^{i}\partial_{p^{i}}\right)\hat{f}(t,k,p)=\hat{\Gamma}(\hat{f},\hat{f})(t,k,p).

Next take the product of (
7.1
) with the complex conjugate of
\hat{f}(t,k,p)
, denoted
\bar{\hat{f}}
, and then take the real part of the resulting equation to obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{d}{dt}-\frac{2\mathfrak{q}}{t}p^{i}\partial_{p^{i}}\right)|\hat{f}(t,k,p)|^{2}=\real{\bar{\hat{f}}\hat{\Gamma}(\hat{f},\hat{f})}.

Next integrate the above with respect to
p
to obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{d}{dt}+\frac{6\mathfrak{q}}{t}\right)\|\hat{f}\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}(t,k)=\real{\left\langle{\hat{\Gamma}(\hat{f},\hat{f})},{\hat{f}}\right\rangle}.

From (
1.49
) this becomes

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\left(t^{6\mathfrak{q}}\|\hat{f}\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}(t,k)\right)=t^{6\mathfrak{q}}\real{\left\langle{\hat{\Gamma}(\hat{f},\hat{f})},{\hat{f}}\right\rangle}.

We integrate in time over
[1,t]
to obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}t^{6\mathfrak{q}}\|\hat{f}\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}(t,k)\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \frac{1}{2}\|\hat{f}_{1}\|_{L^{2}_{p}}^{2}(k)+\int^{t}_{1}\tau^{6\mathfrak{q}}\real{\left\langle{\hat{\Gamma}(\hat{f},\hat{f})},{\hat{f}}\right\rangle}d\tau.

Take the square root on both sides and use (
4.24
) to obtain

\displaystyle t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\|\hat{f}\|_{L^{2}_{p}}(t,k)\leq 2\left\{\|\hat{f}_{1}\|_{L^{2}_{p}}(k)+\left(\int^{t}_{1}\tau^{6\mathfrak{q}}\left|{\left\langle{\hat{\Gamma}(\hat{f},\hat{f})},{\hat{f}}\right\rangle}\right|d\tau\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\}.

We take
\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}
on both sides and then integrate the result with respect to
d\Sigma(k)
over
\mathbb{Z}^{3}
to obtain

\displaystyle{\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}}f\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}\leq 2\left\{\big\|f_{1}\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{p}}+\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\left(\int^{T}_{1}\tau^{6\mathfrak{q}}\left|{\left\langle{\hat{\Gamma}(\hat{f},\hat{f})},{\hat{f}}\right\rangle}(\tau,k)\right|d\tau\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}d\Sigma(k)\right\}.

We now estimate the non-linear term in the upper bound.

From (
5.10
) we have

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle{\hat{\Gamma}(\hat{f},\hat{f})(k)},{\hat{f}(k)}\right\rangle\right|\lesssim\big\|\hat{f}(k)\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}}\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\|\hat{f}(k-l)\|_{L^{2}_{p}}\big\|\hat{f}(l)\big\|_{L^{2}_{p}}\,d\Sigma(l).

We thus conclude that

\tau^{6\mathfrak{q}}\left|\left\langle{\hat{\Gamma}(\hat{f},\hat{f})(\tau,k)},{\hat{f}(\tau,k)}\right\rangle\right|\\
\lesssim\tau^{-3\mathfrak{q}}{\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\hat{f}(k)\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}}{\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\hat{f}(k-l)\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}{\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\hat{f}(l)\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}\,d\Sigma(l).

Then for
\mathfrak{q}>\frac{1}{3}
we have the following uniform estimate

\left(\int^{T}_{1}\tau^{6\mathfrak{q}}\left|{\left\langle{\hat{\Gamma}(\hat{f},\hat{f})},{\hat{f}}\right\rangle}(\tau,k)\right|d\tau\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
\lesssim{\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\hat{f}(k)\big\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}}{\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\hat{f}(k-l)\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}{\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\hat{f}(l)\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}\,d\Sigma(l)\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

This holds because if
\mathfrak{q}>\frac{1}{3}
then we have

\displaystyle\left|\int^{T}_{1}\tau^{-3\mathfrak{q}}d\tau\right|\leq\frac{1}{3\mathfrak{q}-1}\left|T^{1-3\mathfrak{q}}-1\right|\leq\frac{2}{3\mathfrak{q}-1}.

Now we apply the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality with a small constant
\eta>0
to obtain

\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\left(\int^{T}_{1}\tau^{6\mathfrak{q}}\left|{\left\langle{\hat{\Gamma}(\hat{f},\hat{f})},{\hat{f}}\right\rangle}(\tau,k)\right|d\tau\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\,d\Sigma(k)\leq C{\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}}{f}\big\|^{\frac{3}{2}}_{L^{1}_{k}L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}\\
\leq\eta{\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}}{f}\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}+C_{\eta}{\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}}{f}\big\|^{2}_{L^{1}_{k}L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}.

We conclude for some constant
C_{0}>0
that we have

\displaystyle{\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}}f\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}\leq C_{0}\left\{\big\|f_{1}\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{p}}+{\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}}{f}\big\|^{2}_{L^{1}_{k}L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}\right\}.

Thus for
\mathfrak{q}>\frac{1}{3}
and for
\big\|f_{1}\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{p}}
chosen sufficiently small, then
{\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}}f\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}
will remain small for a short time. Thus we obtain the following uniform estimate

\displaystyle{\big\|t^{3\mathfrak{q}}f\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{p}}}\leq 2C_{0}\big\|f_{1}\big\|_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{p}}.

This provides the global uniform decay estimate for any
\mathfrak{q}\in(\frac{1}{3},1]
. Finally, global-in-time existence and uniqueness of the solution follows by a standard continuity argument.
∎

Remark 7.3
.

Let
\mathfrak{q}\in[0,1]
, and recall the weight
\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{q}}(t)
defined in (
6.10
)–(
6.11
). Then, we have the following invariant quantity for the massless Boltzmann equation,

(7.2)

|p^{\prime}|\left|x+\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{q}}(\tau)\left(\frac{p}{|p|}-\frac{p^{\prime}}{|p^{\prime}|}\right)\right|^{2}+|q^{\prime}|\left|x+\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{q}}(\tau)\left(\frac{p}{|p|}-\frac{q^{\prime}}{|q^{\prime}|}\right)\right|^{2}\\
=|p||x|^{2}+|q|\left|x+\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{q}}(\tau)\left(\frac{p}{|p|}-\frac{q}{|q|}\right)\right|^{2}.

Then, in the whole space
\mathbb{R}^{3}
, the invariant (
7.2
) can be used to quantify the dispersion and prove a global-in-time a priori estimate for near vacuum initial data in the style of
[
43
]
and many subsequent works. In this direction, we refer to
[
32
]
.

Appendix A
Conservation laws and H-theorem

In this appendix we show the conservation in time of mass, energy, and momentum, and also the H-theorem. We first define the symmetrised collision operator

Q^{*}(F,G):=\frac{t^{3\mathfrak{q}}}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}v_{\text{\o}}\sigma\Big(F(p^{\prime})G(q^{\prime})+F(q^{\prime})G(p^{\prime})-F(p)G(q)-F(q)G(p)\Big)d\omega dq.

Note that the collision term satisfies
Q(F,F)=Q^{*}(F,F)
.

Lemma A.1
.

For any sufficiently regular functions
\varphi(p)
,
F(p)
, and
G(p)
decaying at infinity, we have

2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{p}}Q^{*}(F,G)\varphi dp\\
=t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}v_{\text{\o}}\sigma\big(F(p^{\prime})G(q^{\prime})+F(q^{\prime})G(p^{\prime})\big)\begin{pmatrix}\varphi(p)\\
\varphi(q)\\
-\varphi(p^{\prime})\\
-\varphi(q^{\prime})\end{pmatrix}d\omega dqdp\\
-t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}v_{\text{\o}}\sigma\big(F(p)G(q)+F(q)G(p)\big)\begin{pmatrix}\varphi(p)\\
\varphi(q)\\
-\varphi(p^{\prime})\\
-\varphi(q^{\prime})\end{pmatrix}d\omega dqdp.

Proof.

The first equation follows by definition. The second follows by using the change of variables
(p,q,\omega)\mapsto(p,q,-\omega)
. For this, we note that
\varrho\sqrt{s}=\varrho^{2}=g(p-q,p-q)
, that under this change of variables
(p^{\prime},q^{\prime})\mapsto(q^{\prime},p^{\prime})
, and the symmetry
\varrho(p,q)=\varrho(q,p)
. Next, in the first equation we use the change of variables
(p,q)\mapsto(p^{\prime},q^{\prime})
, so

\displaystyle 2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{p}}

\displaystyle Q^{*}(F,G)\varphi(p)dp=t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\frac{\varrho^{2}\sigma(\varrho,\theta)}{|t^{\mathfrak{q}}p^{\prime}||t^{\mathfrak{q}}q^{\prime}|}\Big(F(p^{\prime})G(q^{\prime})+F(q^{\prime})G(p^{\prime})

\displaystyle\qquad-F(p(p^{\prime},q^{\prime}))G(q(p^{\prime},q^{\prime}))-F(q(p^{\prime},q^{\prime}))G(p(p^{\prime},q^{\prime}))\Big)\varphi(p(p^{\prime},q^{\prime}))d\omega dq^{\prime}dp^{\prime},

where we used the Jacobian determinant computed in the previous lemma. Moreover, we note that
\varrho
and
\theta
are collision-invariants. We invert the equations to get explicitly
p=p(p^{\prime},q^{\prime})
and
q=q(p^{\prime},q^{\prime})
. Now, we rename
(p^{\prime},q^{\prime})
to
(p,q)
to get

\displaystyle 2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{p}}Q^{*}(F,G)\varphi(p)dp

\displaystyle=t^{3\mathfrak{q}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\frac{\varrho^{2}\sigma(\varrho,\theta)}{|t^{\mathfrak{q}}p||t^{\mathfrak{q}}q|}\Big(F(p)G(q)+F(q)G(p)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad-F(p^{\prime})G(q^{\prime})-F(q^{\prime})G(p^{\prime})\Big)\varphi(q^{\prime})d\omega dqdp.

For the last equation, we use the change of variables
(p,q,\omega)\mapsto(p,q,-\omega)
. Note that under this change
(p^{\prime},q^{\prime})\mapsto(q^{\prime},p^{\prime})
.
∎

Corollary A.2
.

There holds
\int Q(F,F)dp=\int p^{i}Q(F,F)dp=\int|p|Q(F,F)dp=0
for
i\in\{1,2,3\}
. In particular, the mass, momentum, and energy are conserved quantities for solutions of the massless Boltzmann equation on FLRW spacetime.

Proof.

Adding the 4 expressions in the previous lemma, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{p}}Q(F,F)\varphi(p)dp

\displaystyle=\frac{t^{3\mathfrak{q}}}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\frac{\varrho^{2}\sigma(\varrho,\theta)}{|t^{\mathfrak{q}}p||t^{\mathfrak{q}}q|}\Big(F(p^{\prime})F(q^{\prime})-F(p)F(q)\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\big(\varphi(p)+\varphi(q)-\varphi(p^{\prime})-\varphi(q^{\prime})\big)d\omega dqdp.

Therefore, if
\varphi(p^{\prime})+\varphi(q^{\prime})=\varphi(p)+\varphi(q)
, then
\int Q(F,F)\varphi(p)dp=0.
In particular, one can take
\varphi(p)=1
,
\varphi(p)=p^{i}
, and
\varphi(p)=|p|
.
∎

Theorem A.3

(H-theorem for massless Boltzmann on FLRW)
.

For any regular solution of the massless Boltzmann equation on FLRW spacetime, we have

\frac{d}{dt}\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}_{x}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{p}}(-t^{6\mathfrak{q}}F\log F)(t,x,p)dpdx\bigg)\geq 0.

Proof.

Adding the 4 expressions in the lemma above, and letting
\phi=1+\log F
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{p}}Q(F,F)(1+\log F)dp=\frac{t^{3\mathfrak{q}}}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\frac{\varrho^{2}\sigma(\varrho,\theta)}{|t^{\mathfrak{q}}p||t^{\mathfrak{q}}q|}\Big(F(p^{\prime})F(q^{\prime})-F(p)F(q)\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\big(\log F(p)+\log F(q)-\log F(p^{\prime})-\log F(q^{\prime})\big)d\omega dqdp

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{t^{3\mathfrak{q}}}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\frac{\varrho^{2}\sigma(\varrho,\theta)}{|t^{\mathfrak{q}}p||t^{\mathfrak{q}}q|}F(p^{\prime})F(q^{\prime})(1-\mu)\log(\mu)d\omega dqdp,

where in the last line we wrote
\mu=F(p)F(q)F(p^{\prime})^{-1}F(q^{\prime})^{-1}.
And since
(1-\mu)\log\mu\leq 0
for all
\mu>0
, we conclude that
\int Q(F,F)\log Fdp\leq 0.
Finally, we get
